  - 🐛 **`RoomWidgetSettingsUpdateEvent`'s parameters do not line up with its fields.** AS3 takes
    `(type, trax, furni, ui)` and assigns bottom-up: `_uiVolume = param4`, `_furniVolume = param3`,
    `_traxVolume = param2`. Every call site passes
    `(type, trax, furni, generic)`, so the *generic* volume is what surfaces as `uiVolume`. Kept
    exactly — "fixing" the order would silently relabel two of the three sliders.
  - **Three container members and one inventory forwarder were missing**: `soundManager`,
    `messenger`, `avatarEditor` on `IRoomWidgetHandlerContainer`/`RoomDesktop`/`RoomUI`, and
    `IHabboInventory.clubPastPeriods`. The last one is a trap worth recording: there are **two
    distinct `Purse` classes** in AS3 — `catalog/purse/Purse` has `pastClubDays`,
    `inventory/purse/Purse` has `clubPastPeriods`, and only the second is what `HabboInventory`
    holds. The port already uses the right one; several trace comments in
    `inventory/purse/Purse.ts` point at the catalogue path and are wrong.
  - **`HabboHelpTutorialEvent` was constants-only.** AS3 extends `flash.events.Event`, so it has
    always carried a `type`; the port had the four constants and no class body, because nothing
    had raised or received one. `IHabboHelp` and `IHabboMessenger` likewise did not expose the
    event bus their `Component` base has always had.
  - ⚠️ **Two deliberate gaps, both visible as `TODO(AS3)`.** `IID_HabboAvatarEditor` has no ported
    manager (the placeholder `HabboCatalog` and `HabboLandingView` already use), so
    `RWCM_OPEN_AVATAR_EDITOR` logs and raises the tutorial event without opening anything. And
    `roomEngine.selectAvatar()` does not exist — it delegates to
    `RoomObjectEventHandler.setSelectedAvatar()`, an unported mechanism that deselects the previous
    avatar, sends `RoomObjectAvatarSelectedMessage` and consults the wired click handlers. Its only
    effect here is highlighting your own avatar while the menu is open.
  - **`onHabboToolbarEvent()` is decompiler-damaged and ported empty.** AS3 reads the icon id into
    a local and opens an `if("HTIE_ICON_MEMENU" !== id)` whose body is empty — a switch that lost
    its cases. The subscription is kept because AS3 registers it; there is nothing recoverable to
    put inside, and inventing a body would be a guess.
  - **The AS3 `!disposed` guards could not be ported.** AS3 guards each unsubscribe with
    `!manager.disposed && manager.events`; none of this port's manager interfaces declares
    `disposed` (it lives on `Component`, which they do not extend). The optional chain on `events`
    covers the same case — the guard only ever protected a nulled Flash dispatcher.

- 🔄 **Priority 1, slice 16: `RWE_UI_HELP_BUBBLE` — the guided-tour help bubbles**, 2026-08-07.
  `UiHelpBubbleWidgetHandler` (79 l. AS3) + the 4-file `uihelpbubbles/` cluster (836 l.), plus
  `RoomWidgetScriptProceedMessage`, and `friendBarView`/`toolbar` on `RoomUI`. `handler/` goes
  **40/47 → 41/47**; `widget/uihelpbubbles/` goes 0/4 → **4/4**.
  - **It is not driven by room events at all.** The widget registers itself as an
    `ILinkEventTracker` for the `helpBubble/` prefix in its own constructor, and a server-side
    script drives it with `helpBubble/add/<element>/<textKey>/…` links. That is why the handler's
    `processEvent()` is empty: its only job is `sendScriptProceed()` when the last bubble closes.
  - **The element is found by asking four owners in a fixed order** — toolbar, friend bar, room
    tools, then the chat input as a special case — and the first that knows the name wins. A
    bubble whose element *nobody* knows is silently skipped and the tour continues, which is the
    recursive `addNextBubble()` in the else branch.
  - 🐛 **Two AS3 defects kept, both documented at the declaration.** `get type()` returns
    **"RWE_ROOM_POLL"**, not "RWE_UI_HELP_BUBBLE" — AS3's copy-paste, and the third handler to do
    it. And `getWidgetMessages()` registers `RWPM_ANSWER`, the *same string*
    `RoomWidgetPollMessage.ANSWER` uses, so both handlers are called for it and each is
    disambiguated only by its own `instanceof` failing. `PollWidgetHandler` already carried a note
    predicting exactly this; probe-confirmed that a poll answer does **not** reach
    `sendScriptProceed()`.
  - ⚠️ **`FBE_BAR_RESIZE_EVENT` was ported but never wired** — a third instance of the dominant
    failure mode. `HabboFriendBarView` emits it, `RoomUI.bottomBarResizeHandler()` handles it, and
    nothing connected them: the handler was only ever reached through the explicit
    `triggerbottomBarResize()`. AS3 attaches the listener on the `IIDHabboFriendBarView` dependency
    (RoomUI.as:532-538), which is the declaration this slice had to add anyway.
  - **`RoomUI.get toolbar()` was missing too**, though AS3 declares it at RoomUI.as:1270 — the
    field existed and was used internally, with no accessor.
  - **The icon enum needed a guarded lookup.** AS3 reads the constant straight off the class
    (`UiHelpBubbleIconEnum[token]`); doing that in JS also resolves `name`, `length` and
    `prototype`, so a script naming an element "name" would be handed the string
    "UiHelpBubbleIconEnum". Probe-confirmed: the naive form gives exactly that, the explicit table
    gives null and the raw token is used instead — which is the AS3 fallback.
  - **Probe-verified** (browser, real classes resolved through the modules' own specifiers):
    link-tracker registration and pattern; the sibling widgets pulled off the desktop; pair
    parsing incl. a too-short link and an odd trailing argument; the lookup order; and every
    branch of `checkElementPosition()` — balloon above mid-screen (520,265 / arrow "down"), flipped
    below near the top (520,110 / arrow "up", offset 20÷3), pinned left (x=10, offset −70), pinned
    right (x=900, offset 50), and the chat-input path (390,520) which is the only one where the
    initial −1 pointer offset survives. Every number re-derived by hand from the AS3 arithmetic.

- 🔄 **Priority 1, slice 15: `RWE_EXTERNAL_IMAGE` — the photo / selfie wall item**, 2026-08-07.
  `ExternalImageWidgetHandler` (162 l. AS3) + `ExternalImageWidget` (758 l. AS3), plus
  `IRoomEngine.getObjectsByCategory()` and `RoomUI.get habboHelp()`, which the port lacked.
  `handler/` goes **39/47 → 40/47**; both measurements re-run and agreeing at 40.
  - **The only widget opened from two unrelated sources.** `RETWE_OPEN_WIDGET` for a photo hanging
    in the room, and `ROSM_USE_PRODUCT_FROM_INVENTORY` for one still in the inventory — the second
    reads its JSON off the item's stuff data because there is no room object to read a model from.
    The AS3 `processEvent()` does **not** return after the inventory branch; it falls through to
    `as RoomEngineToWidgetEvent`, which is null for a use-product event (the two are siblings under
    `RoomEngineObjectEvent`), so the method exits there. Kept as written.
  - **It is a browser, not a viewer.** Next/previous walk every wall item of the *exact same type*
    in the room, wrapping both ways — so a small poster never browses into large ones and a selfie
    never browses into posters. That is what `getObjectsByCategory()` was needed for.
  - **The content is not in the furni.** `readFurniJson()` finds either an inline photo blob or an
    `id`; an id means a second, unauthenticated fetch to the extra-data service, whose **403** is
    the signal that the photo was moderated away — and the notice is shown only to someone who
    could have removed it anyway. An empty 200 body is "nothing to show", not an error.
  - 🐛 **Two dead members kept on purpose, both AS3's.** `reportWindow` is destroyed in `dispose()`
    and read by `onReportWindowEvent()` but **never assigned** — the in-widget report dialog was
    replaced by `IHabboHelp.startPhotoReportingInNewCfhFlow()` and only its construction was
    removed. `_SafeStr_11415` is declared with the image loader's type and never touched at all.
    Both ported with the reason at the declaration rather than dropped silently.
  - **Two Flash primitives adapted, marked `TS-only`.** The 1px black outline is AS3 drawing the
    photo four times through a black `ColorTransform` at (0,1),(1,0),(1,2),(2,1) then once at
    (1,1); this port composes the same five draws on an `OffscreenCanvas` with `source-in`.
    Verified in a browser probe: a 4×4 red photo yields a 6×6 bitmap, black opaque on the top and
    left edges, red at the centre, **transparent at the corner** — a plus, not a square, exactly as
    the four offsets specify. `stage.stageWidth/Height` becomes the desktop window's size.
  - ⚠️ **One member is a visible TODO, not a silent omission.** AS3 drops a bare Flash `TextField`
    into `name_copy_wrapper` for room controller level 5 only, so staff can select and copy the
    photographer's name. This port has no `TextField`; a holder object keeps the text assignments
    faithful and is handed to `setDisplayObject()` at the same point, but nothing renders it. The
    DOM input bridge is the closest existing mechanism. Non-staff are unaffected.
  - **Probe-verified** (browser, fake window tree, real classes resolved through the modules'
    own specifiers so `instanceof` compares the same class objects): all 23 layout child names
    resolve; `getType()` over the four furni types; `getJsonValue()` short-key-wins-with-empty-
    falls-back; `getImageUrl()` absolute vs bare and selfie-path vs poster; `readFurniJson()`
    routing incl. the malformed blob being swallowed; wraparound in both directions; the three
    `processEvent()` destinations; `deleteCard()`; and the outline pixels above.

- 🔄 **Priority 1, slice 14: the badge-display / achievement-resolution trophy**, 2026-08-07.
  `FurnitureBadgeDisplayWidgetHandler` (331 l.) + `AchievementResolutionTrophyFurniWidget` (150) +
  `RoomWidgetAchievementResolutionTrophyDataUpdateEvent` (94), plus the support layer they needed:
  `BadgeRarityEnum` (134), `BadgeLeaderboardUtils` (81) and the badge-info wire pair.
  - **One widget, two callers, one asynchronous.** The achievement resolution has everything in
    the furni's own stuff data and draws immediately; the badge display needs the badge's
    **rarity**, which only the server knows, so it parks five `_pending*` fields, fires a request
    and finishes when the answer arrives. A resolution arriving mid-flight clears the pending
    request, and the stale answer is then dropped by the badge-code re-check.
  - **The colour work is the point.** A standalone tier's background is its display colour
    lightened (more for rarer tiers, so it stays pale), and its header is the *silver* trophy
    header **multiplied** by that same tint — which keeps the header's shading while taking the
    tier's hue. Unique (6) is the exception: it gets the plain gold header and a white background,
    so the rarest badge is the one that is *not* tinted.
  - **Two new headers, both from WIN63's registry alone.** `GetBadgeInformationComposer` 3159
    (`_composers[3159]`) and `BadgeInformationEvent` 1153 (`_SafeStr_4546[1153]`). The emulator
    implements neither; its only 3159 is an unrelated *server→client* composer, which is a
    different table and so not a conflict. Both class names are **derived** — the identifiers
    exist in no tree, and unlike their `badges/` siblings there is no `win63_version` counterpart
    to recover them from.
  - **`BadgeRarityEnum`'s constants and keys disagree**, and that is AS3's: `VERY_RARE` (3) maps to
    `badge.rarity.epic`. Three of the seven constant names are derived from their own keys.
  - **The event's `color` is `theme + 1`** because the widget derives the theme back out as
    `color - 1` when no explicit theme is given — that is how one event serves both callers, with
    the achievement path sending only the first four fields and getting a plain gold trophy.
  - **One AS3 dead read kept visible:** the widget assigns `viewType` and reads it into a local
    that is never used — a second view type that was never built. Kept as a `void` read rather
    than dropped, so the intent stays legible.
  - **Verified in a browser**: the rarity enum's labels, standalone tests and glow colours behave
    across the uncommon flag; the leaderboard helpers page/format/cap correctly; the parser reads
    owner count **before** rarity; the resolution path sends nothing and draws gold/white; the
    badge-display path draws nothing and sends `["ACH_Test1"]`; an answer for a different badge is
    dropped; very-rare yields silver theme with a tinted header (4286225356) and lightened
    background (4288402175); unique yields gold, gold header and white; common yields the plain
    silver header, white, and **no** owner line at 0; an owner count of 5000 is hidden too; a
    resolution mid-flight makes the late answer inert; the failure case raises the alert with
    `help_error_state`; and the widget applies all four defaults when the extension fields are
    absent, takes them verbatim when present, and disposes the previous view first.
  - 📏 **`handler/` 26/47 → 27/47** — 6 widget types left in priority 1.

- 🔄 **Priority 1, slice 13: the furni chooser**, 2026-08-07.
  `FurniChooserWidgetHandler` (211 l.) + `FurniChooserWidget` (160) + `FurniView` (289) +
  `FurniChooserTableObject` (68). The `chooser/` package from slice 12 carried the rest, so this
  needed only its own four files — **`chooser/` is now 8/8**.
  - 🐛 **The search filter was decompiler-damaged, and was repaired against its intact
    sibling.** `FurniView.populateWithFilters()` comes back as
    `do { for each(token in tokens) {} ... break; } while(item.lowerCaseName.indexOf(token) != -1)`
    — an empty `for each` body, an unconditional `break`, and the real test stranded in the
    `while`. This is the fifth time this project has hit that exact shape. `UsersView` is the
    undamaged sibling and shows the intended structure; rebuilt as an **every-token** match, which
    is the only thing the `split(" ")` and the stranded `indexOf` can together mean.
  - **It maintains its list incrementally, unlike the user chooser.** A removal is spliced out
    locally (matched on id **and** category, since a floor and a wall item can share an id); an
    addition asks the handler for that *one* item and waits for
    `RWCCE_FURNI_CHOOSER_CONTENT_ADD`. A room with hundreds of furni would make the user chooser's
    full-refresh approach unusable. The add is idempotent through an id set, and does **not**
    re-sort — a newly placed item sits at the bottom until the next full refresh.
  - **Name resolution is the handler's real work**: the model carries only a type id, so the name
    comes from the session's furniture data, with the object's type string as the fallback. Wall
    items branch first on posters, whose number is parsed out of the type string and turned into a
    `poster_<n>_name` key — and the wall branch requires a *non-empty* localized name where the
    floor branch takes whatever the data gives.
  - **Items with a non-positive id are dropped** by the widget, which is how the engine's
    placeholders for furni still loading stay out of the list.
  - **`_ignoreListeners` is real here**, unlike in `UsersView` where it is never set:
    `constructOwners()` raises it while repopulating the owner dropdown so the resulting
    `WE_SELECTED` cannot re-filter mid-rebuild. The dropdown is also disabled and half-faded when
    there are fewer than two owners to choose between.
  - **Verified in a browser**: the handler lists both categories in order with names resolved four
    different ways (furniture data, type fallback, poster localisation, wall data); a top-up for a
    missing object emits nothing; selecting acts on categories 10 and 20 but not 100; the owner
    cell shows "-" for a null owner and for a Builders Club id while the identifier is
    `category-id`; the window opens at (690, 10) with the owner menu built from the items;
    "red sofa" matches one item and "red chair" matches none — **every** token must hit; clearing
    restores all three; the owner dropdown narrows to Alice's two; an incremental add appends
    without re-sorting and a duplicate add redraws nothing; a removal splices locally without
    asking the room, and a removal with the wrong category does nothing; an addition asks
    `RWRWM_FURNI_CHOOSER_ADD` for that one id; and everything is inert once the window is closed.
  - 📏 **`handler/` 25/47 → 26/47** — 7 widget types left in priority 1.

- 🔄 **Priority 1, slice 12: the user chooser**, 2026-08-07.
  `UserChooserWidgetHandler` (152 l.) + `UsersChooserWidget` (145) + `UsersView` (249) +
  `UsersChooserTableObject` (59) + the shared `ChooserItem` (80) / `ChooserWidgetBase` (33) /
  `RoomWidgetChooserContentEvent` (40). The `chooser/` package is the last shared backbone in
  `widget/`, so the furni chooser now needs only its own three files.
  - **It reads the room engine, not the session's user list** — the engine knows who is actually
    *rendered*, which is what a chooser is for. A room object with no user data yet is skipped, so
    an avatar still loading stays out.
  - **The table-view backbone it needs was already ported** for the wired menu, so this reuses
    `TableView`/`TableColumn`/`ITableObject` rather than adding anything.
  - **`ChooserItem.owner` is not a field.** Two id ranges override it, so a Builders Club or
    wired-temporary item reports its provenance instead of whoever placed it — and both labels are
    hardcoded English in AS3, not localisation keys.
  - **The refresh is delayed by 100ms on purpose.** A user arriving fires `RWROUE_USER_ADDED`
    before the room object reaches the user-data manager, so asking immediately would miss the very
    user who just arrived. It also only refreshes while the window is open.
  - **Two AS3 oddities kept:** `compareItems()` **never returns 0** — equal names, an empty name or
    a null item all return 1, making that sort unstable (the widget re-sorts properly afterwards,
    which is presumably why nobody noticed); and `_ignoreListeners` is checked by all three input
    handlers but never *set*, so the guard is permanently false.
  - **One contract difference recorded rather than papered over:** AS3's `getTableCell()` returns
    **null** for an unknown column, but this port's `ITableObject` declares it non-null and
    `TableRowView` passes the cell straight on. The default returns an empty cell instead; the
    branch is unreachable either way, since the table only asks for the columns it was initialised
    with.
  - **`activeRoomHasChooserDisabled`** added to `RoomEngine`/`IRoomEngine` — a room variable a room
    can set to opt out of being listed, and the sibling of the already-ported
    `activeRoomHasFreeFurniMovementsMode`.
  - **Verified in a browser**: `owner` returns "Builders Club"/"Temp (Wired)" for the two id ranges
    and the real owner otherwise; the handler lists three users out of four room objects, sorted,
    and reports `chooser_disabled`; selecting acts only on the user category and drops a foreign
    object; the window opens at (690, 10) against its parent's top-right with the amount indicator
    at 3; searching "o" narrows to Bob and Zoe **without asking the room again**, and toggles the
    clear button against the placeholder; clearing restores all three; the type dropdown filters to
    type 2, and selection 3 remaps to type 4 and yields nothing; a user arriving refreshes after
    100ms and not immediately, and not at all once the window is closed; closing resets `state` to
    0; `initialize(1)` asks for the list and `initialize(0)` does not.
  - 📏 **`handler/` 24/47 → 25/47** — 8 widget types left in priority 1.

- 🔄 **Priority 1, slice 11: the word quiz**, 2026-08-07. `WordQuizWidgetHandler` (147 l.) +
  `WordQuizWidget` (422) + `WordQuizView` (224) + `RoomWidgetWordQuizUpdateEvent`. Another case
  where the whole pipeline below it was already ported — three parsers, the session event and its
  handler — and only the widget layer was missing.
  - **The one handler that changes the room itself.** On an answer it makes the voter's avatar
    smile or frown (`updateRoomObjectUserGesture`, AS3's `updateObjectUserGesture`), which is what
    makes a quiz readable at a glance. It also **returns without dispatching** when the voter is
    not in the room — so their vote updates no counts either, not just no gesture.
  - **The signs are the bulk of it.** Each answer raises a thumb window keyed
    `<pollId>_<userId>_<layout>` that follows its avatar on a 40ms timer and fades in, holds, then
    fades out while drifting up — all from a single per-sign countdown, no tween. The user id is
    recovered by **splitting the window's own name** rather than being stored.
  - **Four AS3 oddities kept rather than smoothed:** the placement offsets differ between the
    first frame (+20, -20) and every later one (+29, -11); `poolWindow()` does not pool anything
    (the two pools it implies are only ever filled by `dispose()`, so never read); losing an
    avatar `return`s out of the whole per-sign loop instead of continuing; and `removeWindow()`
    **adds** the desktop resize listener where it meant to remove it, and bails when the window has
    no children.
  - **`_countdown = 4` is assigned then immediately overwritten** whenever a duration is given, so
    it only survives for a question with no duration — where nothing decrements it either. Kept.
  - **Two deliberate deviations:** AS3 *throws* "Failed to construct window from XML!" in both
    `createWindow()` and the measuring path. Both are reached from a server event, and a throw
    there takes the room UI down.
  - **Verified in a browser**: a like gestures `7:42:1` and a dislike `7:42:4`; an absent voter
    dispatches nothing at all; the question strip shows the topic, caps its width at
    `min(660, measured + 6)`, starts the countdown at `duration / 1000` and centres on its *first
    child's* width (x=200, y=6); voting sends `RWPM_ANSWER` with the poll id, question id and
    `["1"]`, then swaps to the result strip; a second vote sends nothing but still dismisses; the
    sign is named `3_555_wordquiz_like_xml`, starts at (120, 180) fully transparent, and after two
    40ms ticks sits at (129, 189) with blend 0.375 — two `FADE_IN_STEP`s; a dislike takes the
    other layout; finishing removes both signs and fills the tallies 5/2; a finish for a different
    question shows no results.
  - 📏 **`handler/` 23/47 → 24/47** — 9 widget types left in priority 1.

- 🔄 **Priority 1, slice 10: the high-score scoreboard**, 2026-08-07.
  `HighScoreDisplayWidgetHandler` (148 l., name **derived** from `_SafeCls_3964`) +
  `HighScoreDisplayWidget` (248).
  - **The first handler that repositions its widget every frame.** It registers itself as an
    update listener from its own `container` **setter** — nothing else does that — because the
    bubble is placed in room coordinates and has to follow the furni as the room scrolls. The
    offset is a flat `(-138, -400)` from the object's screen location.
  - **It needs no server round-trip**: the open case rebuilds the scoreboard from the object's own
    model through `HighScoreStuffData.initializeFromRoomObjectModel()`, which was already ported.
  - **The bubble lives in a full-desktop container of its own**, not in the layout manager — so
    `mainWindow` returns something here, unlike most of the recent slices.
  - **A quiet AS3 detail worth keeping:** `_openEvent` is never cleared on close. What stops a
    stale one driving anything is that `close()` resets the widget's room/object ids to -1, and
    `update()` requires all four of event, open bubble, matching room and matching object.
  - **Five score-type postfixes against three declared constants.** `fastesttime` and
    `longesttime` (indices 3-4) have no `SCORETYPE_*` constant in any tree, which is why the time
    formatting is selected by `indexOf("time") >= 0` on the postfix string rather than by a
    comparison. Ported as-is.
  - **`ROWRE_HIGH_SCORE_DISPLAY` / `ROWRE_HIDE_HIGH_SCORE_DISPLAY`** added to
    `RoomEngine.handleObjectWidgetRequestEvent()`.
  - **Verified in a browser**: the container is created as type 4 and sized to the desktop
    (1024x768); the template row is pulled out of the list before any entry is added; two entries
    render with `"Alice, Bob"` and `01:35` / `1:02:05` — AS3's asymmetric padding, where only the
    first two fields zero-pad, so the hours field is bare; a non-time score type uses the plain
    header and the raw number; an unconfigured furni (both types -1) registers **no** caption
    parameters; the bubble lands at (362, 200) from a screen location of (500, 600); a hide for a
    different object is ignored while the right one closes it and resets both ids to -1; the
    reopened bubble keeps the last position; `update()` does nothing once closed despite the stale
    open event; and `dispose()` leaves the update loop it joined.
  - 📏 **`handler/` 22/47 → 23/47** — 10 widget types left in priority 1.

- 🔄 **Priority 1, slice 9: the in-room friend request**, 2026-08-07.
  `FriendRequestWidgetHandler` (134 l.) + `FriendRequestWidget` (199) + `FriendRequestDialog`
  (297), the message and the event.
  - 🐛 **A third bus was missing.** This handler's three processed events come from **two**
    places: the request is a room-session event, but `FRE_ACCEPTED`/`FRE_DECLINED` belong to the
    friend-list component's own bus, so `RoomUI`'s session-event table cannot carry them. AS3
    subscribes to them in `RoomDesktop`'s `friendList` **setter** (lines 430-437) and unsubscribes
    in `dispose()` (633-638) — this port's setter only assigned the field. Ported, so accepting
    from the friend-list window now closes the in-room bubble too.
  - **The first consumer of slice 8.** `FriendRequestWidget.update()` is the one caller of
    `ObjectLocationRequestHandler`'s synchronous reply: it asks where each sender is and hands the
    rectangle to that sender's bubble. A **null** rectangle is how a request from someone who left
    the room gets dropped.
  - **The bubble is built by the first position update, not by the constructor** — so a request
    from someone who is not in the room never draws anything at all.
  - **The only animation in the slice is four lines.** The first placement jumps; after that a
    move of more than 5px eases halfway per frame, which is what makes the bubble trail a walking
    avatar. Hovering or holding a button freezes it, so it cannot walk out from under a click.
  - **The widget joins the update loop only while it holds bubbles** — `checkUpdateNeed()` runs
    after every add and remove, so an empty room costs nothing per frame.
  - **Three AS3 details kept rather than smoothed:** `type` returns `"RWE_ROOM_POLL"`, which is
    not this widget's type — a copy-paste it shares with `PollWidgetHandler` and
    `UiHelpBubbleWidgetHandler`, and harmless because `type` is only matched against an
    open/close-widget target; closing the bubble is **not** declining (it tells the server
    nothing and the request stays pending); and `showProfile()` alone skips the message-listener
    null check its two siblings have.
  - **Verified in a browser**: the session request dispatches SHOW with name and user id while
    both friend-list events dispatch HIDE with only the request id; the two buttons reach
    `acceptFriendRequest`/`declineFriendRequest` and a foreign object is dropped by the cast;
    adding a request joins the update loop at priority 10 and builds nothing; the first update
    asks `RWGOI_MESSAGE_GET_OBJECT_LOCATION` for user type 1, builds on layer 0 and places the
    window at (182, 270) — a jump; the next update to a target 200px away lands at 282 — halfway;
    hovering freezes it there; accepting sends, disposes and leaves the update loop; an absent
    sender is dropped without a window; a remote hide disposes without sending; close sends
    nothing; both profile links raise `RWOPEM_OPEN_USER_PROFILE`, the name underlines on hover and
    the icon swaps 21/22.
  - 📏 **`handler/` 21/47 → 22/47** — 11 widget types left in priority 1.

- 🔄 **Priority 1, slice 8: the object-location request**, 2026-08-07.
  `ObjectLocationRequestHandler` (128 l.) + `RoomWidgetGetObjectLocationMessage` +
  `RoomWidgetUserLocationUpdateEvent`, wired as `RWE_LOCATION_WIDGET`.
  - **The only handler that answers synchronously.** Every other one dispatches on the bus and
    returns null; this one's return value *is* the answer, which is why the event it builds is
    never emitted anywhere. It is also the only handler whose `type` is **null** in AS3, so it can
    never be the target of an open/close-widget event — kept null here.
  - **Another widget-less type**, like the internal link: AS3's factory has no case for it. It
    only works because `createWidget()` now leaves a handler registered when the factory returns
    null (fixed in slice 3-5).
  - **Two branches, one tail.** A user is looked up by `(webId, type)` through the user-data
    manager to get its room index; a game object is addressed by room index directly. Both then
    read the bounding rectangle and the projected screen location and offset them by the room
    view's origin. A user who is not in the room still gets an event, with both fields null — the
    caller tells that apart by the rectangle, not by a null event.
  - ⚠️ **`AvatarInfoWidget` is *not* routed through it yet, deliberately.** It computes the same
    thing inline. AS3 takes `screenLocation` from `getRoomObjectScreenLocation()` — the object's
    own location projected, an avatar's feet — where the inline block uses
    `(left + width/2, bottom)` off the bounding box. Those are near but not equal, so swapping the
    source would move every bubble in a working, recently-shipped feature. `TODO(AS3)` on
    `AvatarInfoWidget.positionView()` with that exact reason; it needs its own before/after check.
  - **Verified in a browser**: `type` is null and the two message types are claimed; a user
    lookup calls `getUserDataByType(555, 1)` then locates room index 42 on canvas 3 with category
    100, and both results come back shifted by the view origin; a game object skips the lookup; an
    absent user yields an event with two nulls while a missing user-data manager yields no event
    at all; a missing view rect returns the unshifted values rather than nothing; a foreign
    message object, an unknown type and a null container are all dropped.
  - 📏 **`handler/` 20/47 → 21/47** — 12 widget types left in priority 1.

- 🔄 **Priority 1, slice 7: polls — and the routing bug that made the last two slices dead**,
  2026-08-07. `PollWidgetHandler` (124 l.) + `PollWidget`/`PollSession`/`PollOfferDialog`/
  `PollContentDialog` (1022 l. of AS3) + `IPollDialog`, the event and the message.
  - 🐛 **Nothing forwarded room-session events to the room desktop.** AS3's `RoomUI` listener
    table routes 20 session event types to `roomSessionEventHandler()`, which hands them to the
    current desktop and through it to every widget handler that claimed them in
    `getProcessedEvents()`. This port had the *dialog* half of that table and not this one — so a
    handler could be built, registered in `_widgetEventHandlers` and **never called**. The
    doorbell and the room queue, both shipped earlier today, were in exactly that state; the poll
    slice needed the same route and is what exposed it. `roomSessionEventHandler()` and its
    20-entry list are ported, so all three work now.
  - 🐛 **The poll parser produced objects the dialog could not read.** `PollContentsEventParser`
    emitted anonymous `{value, text, type}` choices where every consumer reads
    `choiceText`/`choiceType`, and dropped `children` onto a spread copy. Nothing throws on that —
    it renders an empty answer list. The two AS3 DTOs are now ported (`PollQuestion`, `PollChoice`,
    both **derived** names: `_SafeCls_4294`/`_SafeCls_4101`, obfuscated in every tree) and the
    parser builds them.
  - **The NPS branch is the interesting logic.** A poll question can carry follow-ups; after a
    radio answer the dialog keeps the chosen choice's `choiceType` and serves the first child
    whose `questionCategory` matches, instead of advancing. Clearing the parent index is what
    stops one parent yielding twice, and clearing the choice type before each resolve is what
    makes a non-radio answer end the branch.
  - **`ITextWindow` was missing `htmlText`** — declared on AS3's interface (lines 37/107) and
    implemented by this port's `TextController` all along, just never exposed, so no caster could
    reach it. `PollOfferDialog` is the first ported caller that needs it; added.
  - **Four AS3 details kept rather than smoothed:** every offer button is one-shot, so a double
    click cannot both start and reject; "Later" is the only exit that tells the server nothing;
    two dead guards in `answerPollQuestion()` (`length < 0`, and more answers than choices) are
    kept because removing them would be an invented correction; and a duplicate offer logs and
    **re-offers on the existing session** rather than replacing it.
  - **Two deliberate deviations**, both the same reasoning as the doorbell's: AS3 *throws* when a
    poll answer layout is missing and when the window is gone during a text read. Both are click
    handlers, and a throw there takes the room UI down — they warn and render nothing instead.
  - **Verified in a browser**: the parser builds real `PollQuestion`/`PollChoice` with children and
    NPS flag; all three session events dispatch with the right fields and an unknown one does not;
    the three messages reach `sendPollStart/Reject/Answer` and a foreign object is dropped by the
    cast (the `RWPM_ANSWER` collision with `UiHelpBubbleWidgetHandler`); the offer sets both HTML
    texts and grows the window; a duplicate offer rebuilds on the same session; an error is a
    plain alert with no session; content for an unknown id is dropped; answering the radio
    question follows the matching **child** question, then answering that one sends
    `[4,200,['because']]`, shows the thanks alert and removes the session; the cancel confirmation
    builds once however many times cancel is clicked, and confirming abandons the poll.
  - 📏 **`handler/` 19/47 → 20/47** — 13 widget types left in priority 1.

- 🔄 **Priority 1, slice 6: the area-hide furni**, 2026-08-07. `FurnitureAreaHideWidgetHandler`
  (139 l.) + `AreaHideFurniWidget` (358 l.), the first slice in this run with a real window.
  - **The rectangle is picked on the room floor, not in the window.** The widget drives
    `IRoomAreaSelectionManager` — already implemented here for the wired InArea selector — and the
    corners come back through a callback. That callback counts as a settings change, so with
    `AUTO_SAVE` on (it is, in the shipped client) the pick saves itself and the Apply button is
    hidden dead weight.
  - **This handler does not use the widget event bus at all.** The widget hands *itself* to the
    handler in its own constructor, and the handler calls `open()`/`updateStatus()` directly;
    `getWidgetMessages()` returns null and the window sends its own two composers. It also has a
    `container` **getter** as well as a setter, which the widget reads to reach the connection —
    the only handler that does.
  - **Ported: `SetAreaHideDataMessageComposer`, header 1954** from WIN63's registry
    (`_composers[1954] = _SafeCls_3376`), corroborated by the emulator as `SetAreaHideDataEvent`,
    which is where the class name comes from — the AS3 identifier is obfuscated everywhere. The
    on/off toggle needed nothing new: it is a plain `UseFurnitureMessageComposer` (3353).
  - **Three AS3 details kept rather than smoothed:** `selectButton`'s getter calls
    `_window.deactivate()` — on that one getter of seven — so the room, not the window, holds
    focus for the floor pick, and it fires on every `refreshUI()`; the close button **hides**
    rather than destroys, so re-opening skips the rebuild; and the layout misspells the checkbox
    `invisiblity_checkbox`, which the lookup has to match.
  - **`ROWRE_HIDE_AREA` → `RETWE_REQUEST_AREA_HIDE`** added to
    `RoomEngine.handleObjectWidgetRequestEvent()`. The two names genuinely differ across the
    boundary — room-object side says HIDE_AREA, widget side says AREA_HIDE.
  - **Verified in a browser**: all three rights paths open it and no-rights does not; the model's
    four numbers and three `== 1` flags land on the right controls; opening activates the picker
    and highlights the saved rectangle; switching on dims all nine labels and both checkbox sets
    to 0.5 and releases the picker; a repeated state and a state for another object are both
    dropped; a floor pick sends `[55,11,12,13,14,true,false,true]`; the four buttons reach
    `startSelecting`, `clearHighlight`, `UseFurniture[55,0]` and a dead Apply; a non-click event
    does nothing; close hides without disposing and a status arriving afterwards is dropped; a
    checkbox change auto-saves; double dispose is safe.
  - 📏 **`handler/` 18/47 → 19/47** — 14 widget types left in priority 1.

- 🔄 **Priority 1, slices 3-5: the three handler-shaped widget types**, 2026-08-07. Internal link,
  loading bar and conversion tracking — the ones where the "widget" is barely a widget.
  - **`InternalLinkWidgetHandler`** (AS3 `_SafeCls_3630`, 102 l., name **derived** from the
    `RWE_INTERNAL_LINK` type it declares — the identifier is in no tree). It has **no widget at
    all**: `RoomWidgetFactory` has no case for it in AS3 either. The link is looked for in two
    places in order — the `internalLink` entry of the object's `furniture_data` string map, then
    the flat `furniture_internal_link` model string — because the editable furni writes the map
    and the fixed one writes the string. `disposed` is `_container === null`; this is the one
    handler with no `_disposed` flag.
  - 🐛 **A widget-less type was impossible before this.** `RoomDesktop.createWidget()` called
    `handler.dispose()` when the factory returned null. AS3 (line 985-988) just returns, leaving
    the handler registered in both tables — which is the entire mechanism behind
    `RWE_INTERNAL_LINK`. The port's version was worse than either option: it nulled the container
    while leaving the handler subscribed, so it kept being called and kept doing nothing. Now
    matches AS3.
  - **`LoadingBarWidgetHandler` + `LoadingBarWidget`** (87 + 163 l.) and
    `RoomWidgetLoadingBarUpdateEvent`. The handler is the only one that re-dispatches its input
    unchanged — the desktop already builds the event, so there is nothing to translate. Hiding
    **destroys** the window; the next show rebuilds it. `mainWindow` stays null: the bar centres
    itself and must not be placed by the layout manager.
  - ⚠️ **The loading bar has no trigger yet, and that is recorded rather than guessed.** AS3 drives
    it from a pending-resource list that the decompilation lost — `init()` reads
    `_pendingResources = []` immediately followed by `if(_pendingResources.length > 0)`, so the
    SHOW branch is dead in the source with no intact sibling to recover the list from. The other
    half, `checkInterrupts()`, also owns `roomSessionManager.startSession()`, which this port
    drives elsewhere; wiring it would start the session twice. Full `TODO(AS3)` on
    `RoomDesktop.init()`.
  - **`ConversionPointWidgetHandler` + `RoomWidgetConversionPointMessage`** (84 l.). Its widget is
    a bare `RoomWidgetBase` with no window — AS3's factory returns exactly that, so the handler is
    the feature. **Nothing in any of the three trees constructs the message**; the handler is its
    only reference, and it is ported because the handler is real and registered.
  - 🐛 **`RoomSession.sendConversionPoint()`'s parameters were named for the wrong fields** —
    `(type, value, extra, category, action)` for what the composer and this handler both call
    `(category, pointType, action, extraString, extraInt)`. Same five slots in the same order, so
    the wire never changed; only the names lied. Renamed on `RoomSession` and `IRoomSession`.
  - **`ROWRE_INTERNAL_LINK` → `RETWE_REQUEST_INTERNAL_LINK`** added to
    `RoomEngine.handleObjectWidgetRequestEvent()`, the one line that was missing between the
    furniture logic and the handler.
  - **Verified in a browser**: the map wins over the flat string, an empty map entry falls back to
    it, a missing map does too, and an empty result follows nothing; `getRoomObject` is called with
    (roomId, objectId, category) in that order; a null object and a foreign event type are both
    ignored; `disposed` tracks the container in all three states. The loading bar passes SHOW and
    HIDE through and drops anything else, builds once and centres twice across two shows, disposes
    on hide and builds a **different** window on the next show, and unregisters cleanly. The
    conversion message forwards all five fields in order, coerces both optional tails to `''`/`0`,
    and rejects a foreign type and a foreign object.
  - 📏 **`handler/` 15/47 → 18/47** — 15 widget types left in priority 1.

- 🔄 **Priority 1, slice 2: the room queue — "you are Nth in line"**, 2026-08-07.
  - **Ported end to end**: `RoomQueueWidgetHandler` (163 l. AS3), `RoomQueueWidget` (211 l.),
    `RoomWidgetRoomQueueUpdateEvent` and `RoomWidgetRoomQueueMessage`, wired at the factory,
    `RoomDesktop`'s handler switch and `RoomUI`. `RoomSessionQueueEvent` and its parser already
    existed and already fired — again only the widget layer was missing.
  - 🐛 **Correction to the above, found while porting the loading bar** (same day): the queue was
    first wired into `RoomUI`'s `REE_INITIALIZED` block alongside every other widget. AS3 creates
    it — and the loading bar — at line 1124-1125 inside `createRoomDesktop()`, **before** `init()`.
    That is not cosmetic: a room you are queued for never initializes, so a queue window built at
    room entry would never exist when it is needed. Moved.
  - **The position is a size, plus one.** The server reports how many people are *already* waiting;
    the handler adds one, because that is where you land. Which queue's size counts depends on club
    membership: with more than one queue open, a club member is counted in the `"c"` queue and
    everyone else in `"d"` (`RoomSessionQueueEvent.QUEUE_TYPE_NORMAL`). That choice also sets
    `isClubQueue`, which is what switches the caption to its `.hc` variant.
  - **Three constants had to be derived and say so at the declaration** — AS3 writes `"c"`, `1` and
    `2` inline. `QUEUE_TYPE_CLUB`, `TARGET_SPECTATOR` and `TARGET_VISITOR`; the same two numbers go
    straight back out through `changeQueue()`.
  - **Two AS3 details kept rather than smoothed:** the club half of the window is shown to people
    who do **not** have Habbo Club (it is the upsell, so the flag is inverted); and leaving or
    switching queues **destroys** the window instead of hiding it — the next status rebuilds it,
    which is what keeps the widget stateless between visits. A `queueSetTarget` outside 1–2 emits
    nothing at all, and no inventory means `hasHabboClub` defaults to *true*, hiding the upsell
    rather than showing it to everyone.
  - `HabboInventory` gains the `clubDays`/`clubPeriods` purse forwarders AS3 has, which is what the
    handler reads.
  - **Verified in a browser**: position is `size + 1` for a single queue (4→5); with two queues a
    non-member is counted in `d` (7→8) and a member in `c` (1→2, `isClubQueue` set); no inventory
    yields `hasHabboClub: true`; an unknown target emits nothing; a foreign message type is
    ignored; the four buttons reach `quit()`, `changeQueue(1)`, `changeQueue(2)` and
    `openClubCenter()`; the caption picks all four localisation variants correctly; the change
    button switches to the queue you are *not* in and disposes the window.
  - 📏 **`handler/` 13/47 → 15/47** — 18 widget types left in priority 1.

- 🔄 **Priority 1 opened: the doorbell, first of the 20 missing widget types**, 2026-08-07.
  - 📏 **The gap was re-measured by widget *type*, not by filename**, because the port renames the
    obfuscated handlers (`_SafeCls_3971` is `RentableSpaceWidgetHandler` here). By type: **20 of
    AS3's 47 handler types have no port handler**, across 22 files — and **not one of those 20 has
    a ported widget either**. Every one is a full slice (handler + widget + wiring), not a
    connection job. That is the honest size of priority 1.
  - **Ported: the doorbell, end to end** — `DoorbellWidgetHandler` (109 l.), `DoorbellWidget`
    (146), `DoorbellView` (158), `RoomWidgetDoorbellEvent` and `RoomWidgetLetUserInMessage`, wired
    at all three points AS3 wires them (factory, desktop handler, and `RoomUI`'s room-entry
    creation, in AS3's own order at line 944). Both layouts already shipped, and the session
    already dispatched `RSDE_DOORBELL` — only the widget layer was missing.
  - **Two AS3 details kept rather than smoothed:** `unregisterUpdateEvents()` does *not* call its
    super where `registerUpdateEvents()` does; and the 51st caller at a full door is **denied**,
    not ignored — the cap is enforced by answering, which is why `deny()` is what enforces it.
  - **One deliberate deviation:** AS3 *throws* when a doorbell layout fails to build. Here it warns
    and returns null — a missing layout is a build problem, and throwing would take the whole room
    UI down with it.
  - **Verified in a browser**: all three session events map to their widget events and an unknown
    one is ignored; the widget message reaches `roomSession.letUserIn` with the right flag and
    returns null; a repeated ring does not duplicate the row; accepting sends `Alice:true` and
    drops it; a doorbell answered **elsewhere** clears the row *without* sending anything; the
    50-cap holds and the 51st is denied (`overflow:false`); `denyAll()` sends 50 denials and
    empties the list; and unregistering stops the widget hearing the bus.

- ✅ **Priority-0 sweep finished — and it found 38 messages the dispatcher could never route**,
  2026-08-06. `habbo/moderation`, `habbo/friendlist` and `habbo/help` swept; **priority 0 is now
  closed**.
  - 🐛 **The whole moderation and help incoming sets were in no header table at all.** 14
    moderation events and 24 help/guide/CFH events were ported, parsed and — for 34 of them —
    *subscribed*, but `HabboMessages` had no entry for their ids, so the dispatcher had nothing to
    route the packet to. The mod tools and the entire guide/call-for-help system received nothing,
    silently. All 38 registered.
  - **How the ids were derived, since none of them could be guessed:** `win63_version` registers
    the same handlers with **unobfuscated class names**, WIN63 registers them obfuscated and owns
    the only authoritative header table. A handler spelled identically in both trees pins one to
    the other. Three could not be resolved that way and are **reported, not guessed**:
    `SanctionStatusMessageEvent` and `GuideSessionInviteRequesterMessageEvent` (no win63_version
    registration under that handler) and `MyCfhReportStatusMessageEvent` (not ported).
  - ⚠️ **The name trap, caught in the act.** WIN63 registers `_SafeCls_1807` under a handler called
    `onMySanctionStatusMessageEvent`, which reads like the port's `SanctionStatusMessageEvent` —
    but its parser exposes a single `sanctions()` *list* where the port's exposes ten
    single-sanction fields. Different message. Header 1746 was **not** assigned to it.
  - **Two moderation subscriptions were missing too**, both now wired: `onCfhTopics` (AS3
    subscribes that one message from *both* help and moderation — help builds the report dialog's
    categories, moderation stores them on the issue manager) and `onSanctions`, whose message
    (`CfhSanctionMessageEvent` + parser, header 1634) had to be ported. `ModerationManager` gains
    the `cfhTopics` setter AS3 has.
  - **A duplicate was avoided by checking first:** `UserClassificationMessageEvent` already
    existed under `incoming/moderation/` and was already subscribed — the earlier sweep missed it
    because the port names the file after the message and AS3 names the handler
    `onRoomUserClassification`. It only needed its header (543). A near-duplicate written in the
    meantime was deleted.
  - 📏 **Final counts:** `moderation` 15/17 registrations (the two missing ones are now wired, so
    17/17) but **8/43 files**; `friendlist` **12/12 and 41/40 files — nothing missing at all**;
    `help` 22/41 registrations, 27/34 files. **No orphan classes in any of the three** — nothing
    defined-but-never-constructed. The remaining gaps are unported features, not wiring.
  - **Verified in a browser**: all 38 ids resolve to the expected class, every one constructs, and
    every one carries its parser. The event table went from 369 to 407 entries with no duplicate
    id.
  - **Then the same filter was run over *every* incoming directory, not just these three.** It
    found 101 ported-but-unregistered classes, of which only **five were also subscribed** — the
    ones that actually cost something. Three are now registered: `IsUserPartOfCompetition` (1148),
    `SecondsUntil` (3620) and `IsBadgeRequestFulfilled` (2121), each resolved through the *landing
    view element* that registers it, because two of the three answer a handler called `onInfo` and
    the handler name alone could not tell them apart. **win63_version lists those three at
    1685/1557/2295 — all stale.**
  - 🐛 **One of the remaining two was a duplicate of a message already ported.**
    `GuideSessionInviteRequesterMessageEvent` carries a `@see` pointing at
    `GuideSessionInvitedToGuideRoomMessageEvent.as` — the same AS3 message, already ported and now
    registered at 776 — and in AS3 `GuideSessionInviteRequester` is a **composer**, not an event.
    Both port handlers were trace-only stubs. The duplicate event, its parser and its subscription
    are removed.
  - ⚠️ **The last one is a revision mismatch, and is deliberately left unregistered.** The port's
    `SanctionStatusMessageEvent` came from win63_version, where the message is one flat sanction.
    The 2026 client's message in that position (`_SafeCls_1807`, header 1746) carries a
    `sanctions()` **list**. Registering the old reader against the new id would misparse the wire,
    so the subscription stays inert behind a `TODO(AS3)` that names the header for whoever ports
    the current shape. **The event table is 410 entries, no duplicate id.**

- ✅ **`habbo/sound` COMPLETE: `furni/`, the last file**, 2026-08-06.
  - **`FurniSamplePlaybackManager` (206 l.)**, constructed by the sound manager on the room
    engine's own event bus — the third and last thing AS3 builds in `init()`. Furniture that makes
    a sound now loads its sample once per object, retriggers it on play, follows its pitch, and
    drops it when the object goes.
  - **Its volume is wired too:** `updateVolumeSetting()` had two `TODO(AS3)`s where AS3 pushes the
    furni channel down to this manager — muting the client silenced music but not furniture.
    Both closed, and both managers now dispose with the sound manager as AS3 disposes them.
  - **The same `dispose()` slip as `TraxSample`**: AS3 never sets `_disposed` here either. Kept,
    and for a sharper reason — the flag *is* read, inside its own load callback, so setting it
    would change behaviour rather than tidy it.
  - **Verified in a browser** against a real decoded sample: the manager subscribes to all four
    room events; an object whose `sampleId` is -1 is ignored outright; a real one loads, registers
    as an update receiver and takes the pitch from its initialisation event; `updateVolume(0.25)`
    reaches the loaded sound; disposing the object unregisters *and* disposes it; a play event for
    a disposed object is harmless; and `dispose()` unsubscribes all four listeners.
    The pitch itself is not readable from outside — AS3 has only `setPitch()`, no getter — so what
    is verified there is that the call path runs and the sound is registered for the update tick
    that drives it.
  - **`habbo/sound` is now 29/29.** It leaves priority 3's list entirely.

- ✅ **`habbo/sound/trax` finished: the sequencer, and Trax songs actually play**, 2026-08-06.
  - **`TraxSequencer` (817 l.) + `TraxSampleManager` (245 l.) + the audio graph.** The last
    `TODO(AS3)` between this port and audible music is closed: `loadTraxSong()` builds a real
    sequencer, `HabboSoundManagerFlash10` constructs the sample manager, and `update()` drives its
    decode budget.
  - **How Flash's streaming maps onto Web Audio.** Flash *pulls*: `Sound.play()` raises
    `sampleData` whenever it wants another 8192 frames. Web Audio has no such event, so
    `TraxStreamNode` (TS-only) inverts it — an `AudioWorklet` drains a queue and posts `need` when
    it runs low, which is what the sequencer answers by mixing another block. Its `starved`
    message is exactly AS3's "Audio buffer under run..." log. The processor is built from a Blob
    URL so the engine stays independent of any one build setup.
  - **Everything above the block boundary is AS3's**, including the two layout modes (cut mode
    rounds a sample's bars to nearest, legacy rounds up past a 12.5% threshold — the same song is
    a different length either way), the last-to-first channel mixing where the highest channel
    *overwrites* and the rest add, the two-stage finish, and both fades.
  - 🐛 **One degenerate case worth knowing:** with no fade-out set, `checkSongFinishing()` still
    raises the fade flag on the last block, and the fade arithmetic then divides by zero — AS3
    would write NaN into its output stream, except the block is past the song's end so its frame
    count is zero and the write is skipped. The port keeps the same guard, and the probe confirms
    zero NaN samples across a whole rendered song.
  - **Verified in a browser, including the real audio graph**: two channels of a two-bar song mix
    to exactly `(16385 + 8193) / 32768 = 0.750061` with both output channels equal; a 4-bar song
    renders 22 blocks of 8192 frames and reports 3.99 s (bars are laid out at 88000 samples but
    measured at 88200 — AS3 declares both constants and uses each in its own place); the complete
    event fires once; `stop()` with a fade-out defers completion and then fires it; a 1-second
    fade-in ramps **0.186 → 0.279 → 0.372 → 0.464 → 0.5** and stops exactly at full level, a
    fade-out falls by the same step; and `play()` through the worklet starts, runs the context and
    advances the play head.
  - ⚠️ **A first probe reported the fade-in dead — the probe was wrong, not the code.** It drove
    `render()`, and AS3 raises the fade flags only in `play()` (line 383); `render()` loops the
    block writer directly. Checked against the source before changing anything.
  - **What is left in `habbo/sound`:** `furni/` (206 l., furniture samples) and, inside the sample
    manager, AS3's `loadNextSong()` queue drain — a failed sample download leaves its song waiting
    rather than moving on to the next queued one.

- 🔄 **`habbo/sound/trax`, part 1: the numeric core**, 2026-08-06. **No audio yet** — the
  sequencer and its audio graph are part 2; this is everything underneath them.
  - **Ported (707 l. of AS3):** `TraxData` + `TraxChannel` + `TraxChannelItem` (a song's structure,
    parsed from the one string the server sends), `TraxSample` (the decode and the two mixing
    operations) and `TraxChannelSample` (the play offset a shared sample cannot hold itself).
  - **The packing is kept, deliberately.** AS3 stores decoded audio in a `Vector.<int>` with two
    16-bit values — or four 8-bit ones — per entry, and the sequencer's mixing arithmetic is
    written against exactly that layout. Unpacking to `Float32Array` would mean rewriting the
    mixer's numerics, which is the one place a silent, *audible* bug could hide. `Int32Array` here,
    with every accumulation forced through `| 0` because AS3's `int` wraps at 32 bits and the
    packing relies on it.
  - **Two AS3 quirks kept and documented:** `TraxSample.dispose()` never sets `_disposed`, so
    `disposed` always answers false and a second call repeats an idempotent body; and it nulls its
    usage array, which would make the one unguarded getter throw — emptied here instead, same
    answer everywhere it is actually read.
  - **Verified in a browser against the arithmetic itself**: the song string parses to 2 channels
    with deduplicated sample ids (including the id-0 silence entry) and its `meta` segment
    (cut mode, tempo 120); a malformed item abandons the rest of the song but keeps the channels
    already parsed, as AS3's early return does; a 16-bit/44 kHz sample quantises **-1 → -32767,
    0 → 1, 0.5 → 16385, 0.25 → 8193** and ignores the right channel entirely; `addSample()` mixes
    where `setSample()` overwrites; the 32-sample fade-out ramps down to silence at the loop point;
    11 kHz averages four frames into one value (**-1, 0, 0.5, 0.25 → -2047**) and writes it four
    times; running past the end zero-fills; and `TraxChannelSample` carries its offset forward
    between blocks.
  - **Part 2 is `TraxSequencer` (817 l.) and the audio graph.** Flash streams through
    `SampleDataEvent` — the runtime pulls 8192 frames at a time; the Web Audio equivalent is an
    `AudioWorklet` fed from a ring buffer, with AS3's "audio buffer under run" logging mapping onto
    the worklet's starvation signal. Until it lands, `loadTraxSong()` stays the documented stub and
    nothing plays.

- ✅ **`habbo/sound/music` finished: both play-list controllers**, 2026-08-06.
  - **`JukeboxPlayListController` (281 l.) and `SoundMachinePlayListController` (429 l.)**, built
    and disposed by the music controller on the room's own furniture events — the `TODO(AS3)`s the
    previous entry left. `IPlayListController` was already declared and, like the music controller
    before it, had nothing constructing it.
  - **Seven more messages**: `NowPlaying` (398), `JukeboxSongDisks` (2257), `JukeboxPlayListFull`
    (949), `PlayList` (1242), `PlayListSongAdded` (2785), `GetNowPlaying` (3707) and
    `GetSoundMachinePlayList` (3633), plus `PlayListStatusEvent`.
  - 🐛 **Two more decompiler-corrupted loops, repaired against an intact sibling.**
    `PlayListMessageEventParser` has both `while(0 < count)` with an untested counter *and*
    `new class_3136(0, 0, null, null)` — the four values it just read thrown away.
    `PlayListSongAddedMessageEventParser` reads the same four fields and is undamaged, which is
    what fixes the order: id, length, name, creator. `JukeboxSongDisksMessageEventParser` has the
    same untested-counter shape. Third time this pattern has appeared (see the badges parser).
  - **The two controllers differ in who drives them, and it shows.** The jukebox is told what is
    playing and how far in — `syncCount` in milliseconds, handed to `playSong()` as seconds — and
    only *requests* the next song so its samples are ready. The sound machine picks its own next
    track, wrapping at the end, and resolves a room's position by walking the list subtracting
    each song's length until the offset falls inside one.
  - **Verified in a browser**: all four headers resolve; the repaired parser reads three entries
    leaving no bytes; **90 000 ms into a 135 000 ms list lands inside the second track with a
    30-second play-head**; the next-entry scan wraps 13 → 11 and steps 11 → 12; an empty list is
    ignored rather than replacing a good one; the jukebox's now-playing sets its state and emits
    `NPE_SONG_CHANGED` on the *sound manager's* bus at priority 0, and a current song of -1 stops
    it; a disc whose song is unknown becomes a placeholder entry (length -1) and is requested; and
    the room events build a jukebox controller, then a sound-machine one, then null it on dispose.
  - **`music/` is now complete except what needs the sequencer** — `trax/` (1,495 l.) is the last
    dependency between this module and audible playback.

- ✅ **`habbo/sound/music` opened: `HabboMusicController`, and the Trax title that needed it**,
  2026-08-06.
  - **Why it existed at all:** `IHabboMusicController` and `ISongInfo` were already declared, and
    `HabboSoundManagerFlash10.init()` carried a `TODO(AS3)` where AS3 builds the controller — so
    `musicController` was permanently null and every caller of `getSongInfo()` got nothing. It is
    constructed now, from the same line AS3 builds it on (`HabboSoundManagerFlash10.as:423`), with
    the room engine's own event bus as its third argument.
  - **Ported: the song-metadata spine.** The 817-line controller — the metadata cache, the
    request queue batched once a second behind it, the song-disk inventory, and the four-slot
    priority stack that decides what a room should be hearing. Plus `SongDataEntry`,
    `SongStartRequestData`, the three sound events, and five messages that did not exist:
    `TraxSongInfoMessageEvent` (2278), `UserSongDisksInventoryMessageEvent` (1930),
    `GetSongInfoMessageComposer` (3130), `GetUserSongDisksMessageComposer` (1685),
    `GetJukeboxPlayListMessageComposer` (1281) — every header from WIN63's registry, every name
    from `win63_version`, which carries the sound messages unobfuscated.
  - 🐛 **The song-info parser reads six fields and drops one.** The order on the wire is *id, an
    unread string, name, data, length, creator*, and the constructor is then called as
    `(id, length, name, creator, data)` — a different order again. Skipping the unread read
    instead of performing it would desynchronise every following song in the batch.
  - **Playback stops exactly where the sequencer would be.** `loadTraxSong()` is a documented stub
    returning null (`habbo/sound/trax`, 1,495 l., unported), so every entry's `soundObject` stays
    null and the play paths return at AS3's own `soundObject == null || !ready` guards. The
    `TODO(AS3)`s that remain are the two play-list controllers (jukebox 281 l., sound machine
    429 l.) and the sample bookkeeping — all sequencer- or play-list-bound.
  - **The Trax song title now resolves in the trade tooltip**, which is what opened this module:
    a disc is named after its song, and if the controller does not know it yet the name is
    requested and the tooltip repaints on `SIR_TRAX_SONG_INFO_RECEIVED`. `HabboInventory` gains
    AS3's required `IIDHabboSoundManager` dependency to carry it.
  - **Verified in a browser**: the three headers resolve; the parser reads the emulator's order
    leaving no bytes; three `getSongInfo()`/request calls for two distinct songs collapse into one
    batched `[2, 101, 102]`; an empty queue sends nothing; the answer fills the cache and fires one
    event per song, with a duplicate answer firing none; and the disk inventory **holds its ready
    event back** while a song it names is unknown, requests that song, and fires once it arrives —
    AS3's deferred dispatch, exactly.

- ✅ **Trading, slice 4: the name-scam warning**, 2026-08-06.
  - **All 5 `namescam/` files ported.** The lookalike detector, its result and warning-data records,
    the controller, and the 315-line warning window — the other trader's name and avatar beside the
    names it resembles, with both ways out **locked for six seconds** so the warning cannot be
    clicked away unseen.
  - **The detector is the interesting half.** Two names match if they can be walked together
    allowing only: an exact character, a case-only difference (≤ 2), a lookalike swap
    (`0`/`O`/`o`/`Ö`/`ö`, `1`/`l`/`I`/`!`, … — free and unlimited), or a skipped `.`/`,`/`:` on
    either side (≤ 2). A name containing anything outside the allowed alphabet is never compared.
  - **One dead guard kept as written.** AS3's `detectNameScam()` opens with `if(!param1 && false)`
    — the `&& false` disables the early-out, so a self-initiated trade is checked like any other.
    That agrees with the constant above it
    (`SHOW_NAME_SCAM_WARNING_FOR_SELF_INITIATED_TRADES = true`) and explains why the constant is
    read by nothing. The port expresses it through the constant rather than "restoring" what the
    parameter name suggests.
  - **`HabboInventory` gains AS3's optional `IIDHabboFriendList` dependency** — the friend half of
    the comparison. Optional there and here, so it cannot deadlock the component.
  - **The class name `TradingNameScamDetector` is DERIVED**, and says so at its declaration:
    `_SafeCls_3934` is obfuscated in every tree including PRODUCTION, since it postdates 2016.
  - **Verified in a browser** against the algorithm: 1 and 2 case changes match and 3 does not;
    1 and 2 skipped small-punctuation marks match and 3 does not, from either side; lookalike
    swaps are free and unlimited (`oooo` ≈ `0Ö0ö`); `B8b` is not a lookalike of `Bob`; identical,
    empty, null and out-of-alphabet names never match; and `detect()` deduplicates its candidates,
    skips the identical name, and hands back copies.

- ✅ **Trading, slice 3: the hover tooltip — `ItemPopupCtrl`**, 2026-08-06.
  - **Ported from the 370-line AS3**: one window re-parented under whichever thumb is hovered,
    the 250 ms open / 100 ms close delays, the left/right placement with the arrow pointing back
    at the cell, the limited-item serial overlay, and the three content shapes (product previewer,
    external image, plain bitmap). `HabboInventory.getItemImage()` came with it — the large
    direction-180 scale-64 render the tooltip shows, which is a different request from the small
    grid icon.
  - **The hover half of `TradingView.thumbEventProc()` is now real**, including the credits tile's
    own text and icon, the poster and Ecotron-box name rules, and AS3's own
    `isExternalImagetype()` helper.
  - ⚠️ **The arrow assets are the `_png` trap again.** AS3 asks for `popup_arrow_right_png`; this
    port registers images under the bare basename, so the suffixed lookup would have returned null
    and the tooltip would have had no pointer, silently. Noted at the lookup — see
    `project_asset_name_no_png_suffix`.
  - **Two deliberate deviations, both noted at the line**: AS3 *crops* an oversized picture into a
    new 180x200 BitmapData (and *scales* an external one through a Matrix); here the bitmap is set
    as it is and the window clamped to the same maxima. Identical for every image under the cap,
    which is every inventory icon.
  - **Verified in a browser**: the constructor throws on a null window or asset library as AS3
    does and starts hidden; a plain item sets the name, centres its picture and drives the serial
    overlay from the stuff data (12/100, hidden again at 0); placement is exact — RIGHT lands at
    `parent.width + BOUNDS_MARGIN` with the arrow at `-arrow.width + 1`, LEFT mirrors it; and the
    delays fire at 250 ms and 100 ms.
  - **Still out on trading**: `namescam/` (5 files), the NFT half (waits on
    `habbo/inventory/collectibles`), and the Trax song title in the tooltip (waits on the sound
    manager's music controller).

- ✅ **Trading, slice 2: the trade window, and the map that had nowhere to host it**, 2026-08-06.
  - **`TradingView` ported (1,053 l. of AS3)** and owned by the model, as in AS3: both windows
    (normal + minimised), the two grids with their per-cell ids, the accept/cancel pair driven by
    the state machine, the 3-second confirm countdown, the silver-fee row, the per-side "account
    disabled" notices and the three alert popups. `IInventoryView` and `CreditTradingItem` came
    with it.
  - 🐛 **`getCategorySubWindowContainer()` returned null unconditionally**, so even a finished
    trade window had nowhere to be hosted. AS3 resolves it — and `getCategoryWindowContainer()`
    and `updateView(category)` — through an `_inventories` map keyed by category name, built in
    `init()`. The map is ported; `furni`, `trading` and `pets` register in it. The port had been
    switching on the category name in two of the three, and returning null in the third.
  - **Three more members were private or mistyped where AS3 has them public**: `GroupItem`'s
    `iconImage`/`iconCallbackId` (the view assigns them when a late icon arrives),
    `FurniModel.createGroupItem()`, and `PetsModel.getWindowContainer()` returned `unknown` where
    its own view returns `IWindowContainer | null` — the widening is what hid it from
    `IInventoryModel`. `PetsModel.selectItemById()` took a number against AS3's string.
  - **A re-entrancy was verified rather than "fixed".** Closing from CONFIRMING sends the cancel
    composer **twice**: `close()` sets CANCELLED, whose setter calls `close()` again, and the
    inner call still sees a non-READY state. AS3 does exactly this; the behaviour is now
    documented at `close()` instead of being tidied away.
  - **Verified in a browser**: the shipped `inventory_trading_xml` carries all 21 tags and names
    the view looks up; the window builds both layouts on first `getWindowContainer()`; an accept
    flips the lock icon to `inventory_trading_trading_locked_icon` and the button caption to
    `${inventory.trading.modify}`; the confirmation drives caption/enabled/help through
    countdown → confirm and registers `inventory.trading.countdown:counter=3`; clicking accept
    while CONFIRMING sends `ConfirmAcceptTradingComposer`; and a close from the other side raises
    `${inventory.trading.info.closed}` and leaves the state READY.
  - **Still out**: `ItemPopupCtrl` (370 l., the hover tooltip — shared with the furni and
    collectibles views, so it is its own slice), `namescam/` (5 files), and the NFT half, which
    waits on `habbo/inventory/collectibles`.

- 🔄 **Trading, slice 1 of 2: the whole network side, and a model that now matches AS3**,
  2026-08-06.
  - **The 12 answers are subscribed at last.** Seven were registered and read by nobody; five did
    not exist. New: `TradeOpenFailedEvent` (2855), `TradingOtherNotAllowedEvent` (814),
    `TradingYouAreNotAllowedEvent` (2294), `TradeSilverSetMessageEvent` (1490),
    `TradeSilverFeeMessageEvent` (3497), plus the `AddItemsToTradeComposer` (3370) and
    `SilverFeeMessageComposer` (2717). **All seven headers came from WIN63's own registry** and
    every one is corroborated by the emulator. `win63_version` carries the whole trading message
    set unobfuscated, so no name here is derived.
  - **`TradingModel` rewritten from the AS3, 315 → ~900 lines.** The port had a plausible
    skeleton with an invented `ITradingUser` record and a 4-member `ITradingModel` widened to 12.
    AS3's interface really is 4 members; the model really does hold both sides flat. Now faithful:
    the full 7-state machine with its per-transition side effects (and its two `throw`s), NFT and
    silver-fee state, `handleMessageEvent()`'s dispatch, and every `request*` composer.
  - **Two parsers were renamed to AS3's own accessors**, because the port's names hid the
    asymmetry the code depends on: `TradingOpenMessageParser` was `userOne/userTwo` where AS3 says
    `userID`/`otherUserID` — `onTradingOpen()` *swaps* the two when the second id is ours — and
    `TradingItemListMessageParser` returned two `{userId, items, …}` records where AS3 has eight
    flat accessors. Read order unchanged in both, and nothing consumed the old shapes.
  - 🐛 **`FurniModel.updateItemLocks()` took a parameter AS3 does not have**, so nothing ever
    passed one and no item was ever shown as locked. AS3 computes the list itself from the trading
    model; it does that now, and an offered item greys out in the furni grid. (Its early return
    also skips the action-view refresh, which the port did on both paths.)
  - **Two type escape hatches removed rather than cast around.** `IFurnitureItemData.slotId` was
    `string` where AS3's is a nullable `String` — the trading item parser returns null outright —
    which forced an `as unknown as` at the call site. Widened through `IFurnitureItem` too.
  - **`ErrorReportStorage.setDebugData()` was not the AS3 name.** It is `addDebugData()`, and it
    removes the key before re-adding so the newest entry sorts last in the dump. Renamed, 8 call
    sites updated.
  - ⚠️ **Two emulator-side stubs found while checking the wire.**
    `TradeSilverSetMessageComposerSerializer` and `TradeSilverFeeMessageComposerSerializer` have
    **empty `Serialize()` bodies** in `vortex-emulator`, while the client reads two ints and one
    int respectively — if either is ever sent, the client hits end-of-buffer. Nothing sends them
    today. Also note `Headers.cs` carries placeholder 9014 for `RemoveNftFromTradeEvent` with a
    comment saying no such composer exists in the 701 client: it does, at
    `_composers[521] = _SafeCls_3173`, called from `TradingModel.requestRemoveItemFromTrading()`.
  - **Verified in a browser, driving the real model**: all 12 event headers and 3 composer headers
    resolve to the right classes; the three new parsers read the emulator's exact order and leave
    no bytes; a full trade runs `RUNNING → COUNTDOWN → CONFIRMING → CONFIRMED → COMPLETED →
    READY`, sending `AcceptTradingComposer` then `ConfirmAcceptTradingComposer` and closing
    itself; an accept lands on the side its user id names; `AddItemsToTradeComposer([11,22,33])`
    serialises `[3,11,22,33]`; and an unlisted transition throws, as AS3 does.
  - **Slice 2 is TradingView**, and it needs four unported pieces first: `IInventoryView`,
    `ItemPopupCtrl`, `CreditTradingItem` and the inventory's trading sub-page hosting. The four
    layouts already ship (`inventory_trading_xml`, `…_minimized_xml`, `…_name_scam_warning_xml`,
    `…_wired_xml`). `namescam/` (5 files) and the NFT/collectibles half go with it.

- ✅ **Priority-0 sweep of `habbo/catalog` and `habbo/inventory` — and the badge list nobody
  read**, 2026-08-06.
  - 📏 **The measurement.** Catalog subscribes **31 of AS3's 45** registrations
    (`HabboCatalog.as:707-751`); inventory **17 of 52** (`_SafeCls_1951.as`). No class in either
    module is defined-but-never-constructed — every model (`TradingModel`, `BadgesModel`,
    `BotsModel`, `EffectsModel`, `PetsModel`, `FurniModel`) has a `new` in `HabboInventory`, and
    the one catalog class referenced from nowhere, `MarketPlaceOfferState`, is referenced from
    nowhere in AS3 either. The whole gap in both modules is the subscription list.
  - 🐛 **The badge list was requested and thrown away.** `HabboInventory.requestBadges()` sent
    `GetBadgesComposer`, and `BadgesMessageEvent` — ported, parsed, registered at 2748 — was
    subscribed only by `HabboClubCenter`, for its own club-badge lookup. Nothing fed
    `BadgesModel`, so the inventory badge tab stayed empty until a badge was awarded *live*
    (`onBadgeReceived`, the one badge message that was wired). Now `onBadges` runs AS3's order:
    assemble fragments → `initBadges()` → `updateView()` → `setInventoryCategoryInit('badges')`.
  - 🐛 **And the club centre could never resolve its badge either.** AS3's `addMessageFragment()`
    returns a single-fragment message **before it reads `fragmentNo`**; the port indexed every
    fragment by number and walked `0..totalFragments-1`. `vortex-emulator` numbers a lone fragment
    **1**, not 0 (`BadgesEventMessageComposerSerializer` writes `totalFragments 1, fragmentNo 1`),
    so the loop found nothing at 0 and returned every time. Ported faithfully in both places —
    this is why the helper exists in AS3 at all.
  - **Verified in a browser**: the parser reads the emulator's exact order — `int totalFragments,
    int fragmentNo, int count, then (int slotId, string badgeCode)` — leaves no bytes unread,
    derives `activeBadgeIds` from `slotId > 0`, and `BadgesModel.initBadges()` fills from it;
    the client resolves `BadgesMessageEvent` at **2748**, which is `MessageComposer.BadgesComposer`
    in `Headers.cs`. The two fragment fixes are verified against the AS3 helper and by typecheck,
    not against a live multi-fragment session.
  - **What the sweep leaves open, in order of product weight:**
    - **Trading landed 2026-08-06** (three entries above): messages, model, window and tooltip.
      What remains is the NFT half (waits on `habbo/inventory/collectibles`) and the Trax song
      title (waits on the sound manager's music controller).
    - **Inventory, the rest of the 35:** collectibles (no module), bots add/remove, post-it
      placed, not-enough-credits, badge point limits, `onUserBadges`, marketplace (4 handlers, and
      `inventory/marketplace/` is an empty directory here).
    - **Catalog, the 14:** `onCatalogPublished`, `onLimitedEditionSoldOut`, `onLtdRaffleEntered/
      Result`, `onGiftWrappingConfiguration`, `onGiftReceiverNotFound`, `onProductOffer`,
      `onNftStorePurchase`, `onBundleDiscountRuleset`, `onSilverBalance`, `onEmeraldBalance`,
      `onMarketplaceMakeOfferResult`, `onRoomExit`, `onSnowWarGameTokenOffer` (that last one waits
      on `habbo/game`). None of their message classes exist yet — these are ports, not wiring.
      `sendGetProductOffer()` already carries a `TODO(AS3)` saying its reply is unported: the
      request goes out on every limited-edition page and the answer is dropped.

- ✅ **The rentable-space furniture, end to end — and `FriendlyTime` was rendering hardcoded
  English client-wide**, 2026-08-05.
  - **The slice.** `RentableSpaceDisplayWidget` + `RentableSpaceWidgetHandler` + its six messages
    (status/rent-ok/rent-failed events with parsers, and the status/rent/cancel composers), wired
    into `RoomWidgetFactory`, `RoomDesktop` and `RoomUI`. `habbo/ui/widget/furniture` is now
    **46/54**, `habbo/ui/handler` **25/47**.
  - **All six message names came from AS3, not the emulator.** `win63_version` carries the whole
    `{incoming,outgoing,parser}/room/furniture/RentableSpace*` set unobfuscated; the emulator
    corroborates every one of the six headers (2800/2158/3117, 2626/3165/61). Only the handler's own
    class name is derived — `_SafeCls_3971` is obfuscated in every tree, identified by
    `implements IRoomWidgetHandler` plus its `RWE_RENTABLESPACE` type, and named after that.
  - 🐛 **`FriendlyTime` had lost its localization.** The port's version took no localization manager
    and concatenated English words — `"5 days"`, `"just now"` — where AS3 resolves
    `friendlytime.days` with an `amount` parameter, because word order differs per language. That is
    **9 call sites across 6 modules**: group profiles, room info, quests (×3), the wired monitor,
    the infostand's rent expiry and the club centre (×2). One of them already carried a note saying
    the parameter had been dropped. Now faithful — `getFriendlyTime` / `getShortFriendlyTime` /
    `getLocalization` all take the manager first, as AS3 does, and every caller passes one. Note the
    suffix is appended to the *key*, not the text: `('.ago')` looks up `friendlytime.days.ago`.
  - **A second interface had to admit null.** `IRoomWidgetHandler.getWidgetMessages()` said
    `string[]`, but AS3 returns a null `Array` from this handler (its widget calls it directly
    instead of sending widget messages). Widened to `string[] | null`, `RoomDesktop` spreads `?? []`
    — the same fix `getProcessedEvents()` needed earlier today, for the same reason.
  - **Two AS3 quirks kept rather than tidied**, both noted at the declaration: the container setter
