  - The toolbar's RECEPTION icon (`HabboLandingView.ts::onToolbarClick()`) was a no-op: AS3's `RoomSessionManager` keys every session under one hard-coded slot (`getRoomIdentifier()` always returns `"hard_coded_room_id"`), so its `getSession(-1)`/`disposeSession(-1)` really mean "the current session, whatever it is." The TS port keys sessions by their real room id (`room_${roomId}`, to support multiple concurrent sessions — a deliberate divergence, not a bug), so `-1` never matched anything and the click silently did nothing. Fixed by using `roomEngine.activeRoomId` instead of the literal `-1`. Also restored the `QuitMessageComposer` send that AS3 does before disposing the session (missing from the TS port — meant the server was never told the client left).
- 🟡 **Third room widget ported: chat input (RWE_CHAT_INPUT_WIDGET)**: `RoomChatInputWidget`/`RoomChatInputView`/`ChatInputWidgetHandler` ported from `sources/win63_version/habbo/ui/widget/chatinput/*.as`. Core input box works: create/position (relative to toolbar/room-tools/friend-bar widths), focus, typing indicator (`RWCTM_TYPING_STATUS`), send on Enter with speak/whisper/shout mode-prefix parsing, flood control show/hide. New supporting classes: `RoomWidgetChatMessage`, `RoomWidgetChatTypingMessage`, `RoomWidgetChatSelectAvatarMessage`, `RoomWidgetRequestWidgetMessage`, `RoomWidgetChatInputContentUpdateEvent`, `RoomWidgetFloodControlEvent`, `RoomWidgetUserInfoUpdateEvent`, `RoomWidgetZoomToggleMessage`.
  - `TODO(AS3)` scope cuts (all noted in-code): the NUX first-time chat reminder animation, the room-enter "chat dimmer" overlay, and the help-button hover tooltip are not ported. `ChatInputWidgetHandler`'s ~50-command slash-command switch (`:kick`, `:mute`, `:zoom`, `:fps`, `:cam`, `:wired`, `:reload`, etc. — spanning moderation/wired-menu/room-engine-debug features that don't exist yet) is not ported; only normal speak/whisper/shout chat-sending is wired, slash commands currently pass through as literal chat text. `getFriendBarWidth()` returns 0 (friend bar isn't wired into `RoomUI`, see `habbo/friendbar` row). Custom chat styles (`ChatStyleSelector`/`freeFlowChat.chatStyleLibrary`) **are now ported** — see the `habbo/freeflowchat` row below.
  - **Superseded, not "not started"**: the old chat *display* widget (`RWE_CHAT_WIDGET` → `RoomChatWidget`/`RoomChatItem`/`RoomChatHistoryPulldown`/`RoomChatHistoryViewer`/`ChatBubbleFactory`, ~2900 AS3 lines) is deliberately disabled — `ChatWidgetHandler.processEvent()` early-returns when `container.freeFlowChat` is set, matching primary/2026 source (which removed the old system's user-preference toggle and dropped `RoomWidgetEnum.RWE_CHAT_WIDGET` entirely). The live speech-bubble renderer is now `habbo/freeflowchat` — see that row.
- ✅ **Bottom toolbar: wired 6 dead extensions + tagged remaining gaps**: `HabboToolbar.as::onPerksUpdated()` calls 8 extension-init methods; the TS port only called 2 (`initPurseAreaExtension`, `initSeasonalCurrencyExtension`) — the other 6 extension classes (`SettingsExtension`, `ClubDiscountPromoExtension`, `CitizenshipVipDiscountPromoExtension`, `CitizenshipVipQuestsPromoExtension`, `VideoOfferExtension`, `OfferExtension`) were fully implemented but never constructed. Added the matching `initXExtension()` methods (with their exact AS3 config-flag gates) plus disposal, so all 8 now run. Also added explicit `TODO(AS3)` markers (previously silent/untagged, violating the project's traceability rule) for: the friends list gap in `HabboFriendBar.ts` (see `habbo/friendbar` row above), and the toolbar icons with no click subscriber yet, in `HabboToolbar.ts::toggleWindowVisibility()`.
- 🟡 **First room widget ported: infostand (furni-only)**: `IRoomWidget`/`RoomWidgetBase` foundation, `InfoStandWidget`/`InfoStandFurniView`/`InfoStandFurniData`/`InfoStandCrackableFurniView` fully ported (click furni → info panel; move/rotate/pickup/eject/use/wired-inspect all work end-to-end through `RoomEngine.modifyRoomObject()`/`useRoomObjectInActiveRoom()`), `InfoStandWidgetHandler` scoped to furni message/event cases. `RoomDesktop.createWidget()`/`processWidgetMessage()`/`roomObjectEventHandler()` are wired for real (previously stubs); `RoomEngine` gained click-to-select (`REOE_OBJECT_SELECTED`/`DESELECTED`), `modifyRoomObject()`, `useRoomObjectInActiveRoom()`. User/Pet/Bot/RentableBot/Jukebox/SongDisk sub-views are inert `TODO(AS3)` stubs — next widget-port session should either flesh these out or pick the next `RWE_*` type from `RoomWidgetFactory.as`. Remaining furni-only gap: the Builder's Club "place more from infostand" flow (`InfoStandWidget.as::onRoomObjectPlaced()`, needs a `BuildersClubPlaceRoomItemMessageComposer`/`BuildersClubPlaceWallItemMessageComposer` pair plus a `RWROUE_OBJECT_PLACED` widget-event bridge off the existing `REOE_PLACED` room-engine event) is still `TODO(AS3)` — deliberately deferred since it touches the placement pipeline, not just the info panel.
- ✅ **Infostand furni thumbnail now renders the live placed object**: `InfoStandWidgetHandler.requestFurniImage()` was using the catalog-type icon as a stand-in, with a comment claiming `RoomEngine.getRoomObjectImage()`'s underlying pipeline (`RoomPreviewer.getRoomObjectImage()`) was still an unimplemented stub — that stub was actually already wired for real in an earlier Phase 5 pass, making the comment stale. Switched to `RoomEngine.getRoomObjectImage(roomId, furniId, category, ...)` (reads the object's own current color/extras/state), replicating AS3's scale-64-then-fallback-to-scale-1 retry when the render is null or exceeds the panel's 140x200 slot.
  - Found and fixed the actual blocker while wiring this up: `RoomEngine.getGenericRoomObjectImage()`'s "content already available" branch reset `result.id` to `0` before asynchronously calling `listener.imageReady(objectId, ...)` — so the id returned to the caller never matched the id the async callback actually delivered, and the callback was silently dropped whenever the object's content was already loaded (the common case for anything already visible in the room). Removed the reset so `result.id` and the delivered id always agree; this also fixes the same latent bug in `ProductViewCatalogWidget`/`ClubGiftWidget`'s catalog preview images, which shared the exact same correlation pattern.
  - That fix still left the thumbnail permanently blank: AS3's `handleGetFurniInfoMessage()` calls `getRoomObjectImage(..., listener=null)` — a synchronous one-shot read that bypasses `isRoomObjectContentAvailable()`'s pending-listener gate entirely, safe because AS3 always has a real BitmapData the instant it's called (the object is already rendering live). TS can't do a truly synchronous ImageBitmap capture, so it must pass a real listener — which then hit the pending gate (furniture never satisfies `isRoomObjectContentAvailable()`, that check is 'user'/snowball-only) and waited on a future `contentLoaded` event that, for content loaded long ago, never fires again. Added a TS-only `forceImmediate` param to `getGenericRoomObjectImage()`; `getRoomObjectImage()` passes it whenever it found a live object with a loaded model (by construction, always true for something already rendered on screen), skipping the pending branch and delivering via the async `imageReady()` path immediately instead.
- ✅ **Infostand USE button was permanently unreachable**: found via live console tracing (temporary debug logging in `WindowRendererItem`/`InfoStandFurniView`, removed after diagnosis) after the config flag (`infostand.use.button.enabled`) and controller rights were confirmed correct and the button still never appeared. `RoomEngine.addRoomObjectFurniture()`/`addRoomObjectWallItem()` received `usagePolicy`/`expiryTime` as parameters but never wrote them onto the room object's model (`RoomObjectModel.getNumber()` returns `NaN` for an unset key) — so `InfoStandWidgetHandler`'s `event.usagePolicy` was always `NaN`, and `NaN === 1`/`NaN === 2` are never true regardless of config or rights. Also silently dropped: `addObjectWallItem()`'s `usagePolicy`/`secondsToExpiration` parameters, never forwarded to `addRoomObjectWallItem()` at all (which didn't even accept them). Added the missing `model.setNumber(FURNITURE_USAGE_POLICY/FURNITURE_EXPIRY_TIME/FURNITURE_EXPIRY_TIMESTAMP, ...)` calls to both floor and wall item creation, matching `_SafeCls_90.as`'s `addObjectFurnitureFromData()`/`addObjectWallItemFromData()` exactly — this also fixes rented-item expiration display and the extend/buyout button visibility, which read the same two previously-never-set keys.
- ✅ **Infostand `info_border` was using the wrong border skin (style 2, blank, instead of style 1, dark)**: the currently-compiled `furni_view.xml` (`937__SafeCls_629.xml`, uid `680D3126-...`, present identically in both the primary WIN63-2026 and win63_2023 dumps) declares `info_border` as `style="2"` ("white borderless" — genuinely blank per this port's skin renderer, verified pixel-for-pixel against the real Flash-extracted `habbo_blue_skin.png` atlas). A third source tree (`WIN63-202601121721-391685409`) contains two alternate `furni_view.xml` revisions (different uids, structurally identical to — and a superset of — the currently-ported one, same element names throughout) that both use `style="1"` ("black borderless", a real verified dark-gray `61,61,61` skin in the same atlas) instead. Patched the compiled `furni_view.json`'s `info_border.style` from `"2"` to `"1"` directly, since `sources/` is a read-only reference archive with no override mechanism — documented here since JSON can't carry inline comments. If a future source re-sync regenerates this file from `937__SafeCls_629.xml`, the style will need to be corrected again until that specific source file's `style` value is fixed upstream.
- ✅ **Infostand widget visual bug-fix pass (position/font/cropping)**: found via iterative live testing after the initial infostand port.
  - `RoomDesktopLayoutManager.getWidgetContainer()` matched widget-type strings case-sensitively (`'RWE_INFOSTAND'.indexOf('infostand')` never matched), silently misrouting the widget's window to the wrong/no container.
  - `RoomDesktopLayoutManager.setLayout()`/`addWidgetWindow()`/`getWidgetContainer()` were re-ported from the clean `win63_2023_version/com/sulake/habbo/ui/DesktopLayoutManager.as` (the primary `win63_version/habbo/ui/class_3019.as` has decompiler corruption here: a `null.`-ref infinite loop and a dropped final `return`) — now uses AS3's real tag-based `getWidgetContainer()` (reads the widget window's own `.tags`, not its type-string name) and wires the real `trimContainer()` (`WE_CHILD_RESIZED` → shrink-wrap a single-child slot to that child's size) instead of an invented one-off reposition hack that was tried and reverted.
  - Custom webfonts (`Volter (Goldfish)`, `Ubuntu*`) were bundled but never registered with the browser — `App.ts` now loads them via the `FontFace` API before first render.
  - `ctx.font` strings built with an unquoted multi-word family name (`9px Volter (Goldfish), Ubuntu, ...`) are invalid Canvas 2D syntax and silently drop the whole font declaration with no console error; centralized quoting now lives in `core/window/utils/CanvasFontString.ts::quoteFontFamilyList()`, used by `WindowComposite`, `TextController`, and `TextLabelController`.
  - `TextController`/`TextLabelController` line-height was a flat `fontSize`-based guess (AS3 used Flash's real measured `TextField.textHeight`); replaced with `CanvasFontString.ts::measureFontLineHeight()` using `TextMetrics.fontBoundingBoxAscent/Descent` (the font's fixed design metrics — not `actualBoundingBox*`, which is per-glyph ink and was tried/reverted for being inconsistent across caption strings). Fixes descenders getting clipped (e.g. "My World" rendering as "Mv World").
  - JSON layouts commonly declare text margins as one nested `vars.margins: {left,top,right,bottom}` var (serialized to XML as a `Map`), but `TextController`/`TextLabelController`'s property-application only recognized 4 flat `margin_left`/`margin_top`/`margin_right`/`margin_bottom` keys — the nested form was silently dropped, so every such label rendered with 0 margins (buttons shrink-wrapped via AS3's real `ButtonController` `EXPAND_TO_ACCOMMODATE_CHILDREN`/`WE_CHILD_RESIZED` → `width = 0` behavior ended up tight enough to look cropped/too-narrow). Both controllers now handle a `case 'margins'`.
- 🟡 **Second room widget ported: room-tools (RWE_ROOM_TOOLS)**: `RoomToolsWidget`/`RoomToolsCtrlBase`/`RoomToolsToolbarCtrl`/`RoomToolsInfoCtrl`/`RoomToolsHistory` fully ported from `sources/win63_version/habbo/ui/widget/roomtools/*.as` (no decompiler corruption found there), `RoomToolsWidgetHandler` fully ported. Settings/room-info popup, collapse/expand slide animation, camera button dispatch (`HabboToolbarEvent`), like/rate, share-room popup + clipboard, room-history back/forward navigation, and tag-click-to-search all work. `RoomWidgetFactory`/`RoomDesktop.createWidget()` wired for `'RWE_ROOM_TOOLS'` matching `RoomDesktop.as:885-890` exactly (constructs the handler, sets `.communicationManager`/`.navigator`). Added `IID_HabboNavigator`/`IID_HabboCommunicationManager` DI wiring to `RoomUI`/`RoomDesktop` (previously registered symbols, never consumed here); added `RoomUI.desktop` (AS3's `var_22` is a single field, the TS port already keyed multiple desktops by room identifier in a `Map` for the underlying multi-session architecture — this exposes "the most recently created desktop" as the AS3-equivalent view) and `IRoomDesktop.getWidget()` (was implemented on the concrete class but missing from the interface). Also created the missing `RoomWidgetZoomToggleMessage` (referenced by infostand-era `getWidgetMessages()` docs but never actually added).
  - One remaining scope cut, logged in-code as `TODO(AS3)`: the `room_tools_history_item` layout asset is not bundled, so the visited-rooms **dropdown list** doesn't render (back/forward navigation buttons work fully, they only need the in-memory visited-rooms array). `freeFlowChat` **is now wired into `RoomUI`/`RoomDesktop`** (see the `habbo/freeflowchat` row) — the chat-history *toggle button* itself is still a no-op, since the drag-down history tray it would open (`ChatHistoryTray`/`ChatHistoryScrollView`) is a separate, explicitly-deferred feature.
  - AS3's cross-room widget-instance reuse (`RoomUI.as`'s `var_4627`/`var_1358`: `RWE_ROOM_TOOLS` and 7 other widget types are reconstructed-or-reused via `widget.reuse(newDesktop)` instead of always reconstructing) is not ported — `RoomDesktop.createWidget()` now sets the `widget.reusable` flag correctly per type (previously hardcoded `false` for everything, which happened to also affect infostand — that flag is currently unread/inert either way) but the caller-side instance cache in `RoomUI` doesn't exist yet, so every widget is still freshly constructed each room-enter.
- ✅ **core/window dropdown compact text follow-up**: `WindowComposite` treats `_DROPLIST_TITLETEXT` and dropdown item `_BTN_TEXT` as compact Flash TextField content, with AS3-style metrics/clipping and baseline offset.
- ✅ **Navigator dropdown/create-room input follow-up**: top-level navigator tabs select the AS3 matching search index, dropmenu title text uses compact Flash margins, create-room thumbnails use AS3 interpolated image URIs plus tile label colors, and `TextFieldManager` refocuses the browser input after placeholder clearing.
- ✅ **Navigator create-room/search/dropdown tabs AS3 parity pass**: `RoomCreateViewCtrl` follows WIN63 prepare/refresh/thumbnail/dropdown/create composer flow; navigator tabs avoid converted Flash `y=-1` truncation; search input border clicks focus the text field.
- ✅ **Navigator room usercount label AS3 follow-up**: `RoomEntryElementFactory.updateCommonEntryElements()` keeps the compact `room_usercount` label on the Flash visual baseline after caption updates.
- ✅ **core/window TextField vertical AS3 parity fix**: `WindowComposite.compositeText()` applies the Flash `TextField` top gutter so purse currency numbers and other glyphs no longer render too high.
- ✅ **Window ResourceManager pending asset wake-up**: `registerAssetUrl()` starts lazy loading for receivers that requested a static bitmap before the bundled image URL was registered.
- ✅ **Configuration/localization/gamedata parity passes**: AS3 `external_texts`, `external_variables`, `furnidata`, `productdata`, avatar figuremap, and room content loading behavior were realigned over recent batches.
- 🐛 **Eight AS3-divergence bugs found via targeted audit and fixed**: `RoomPlaneParser.ts` (`hasRightEdge` never recomputed after the height-scan loop, so it reflected the pre-shrink rect width — same bug class as the previously-fixed `hasTopEdge` polarity inversion); `RoomPlane.ts::getTexture()` (dropped the `time` argument to the rasterizer, permanently freezing `LandscapeRasterizer`'s animation timestamp so animated planes re-rasterized every frame instead of every 500ms); `RoomPreviewerWidget.ts` (the `scale`/`offsetX`/`offsetY`/`zoom` setters never called `refresh()`, so changing zoom/pan on the furniture/pet preview did nothing; `onClickRoomView()` was an empty stub instead of forwarding to `changeRoomObjectState()`); `RoomSettingsCtrl.ts` (`parseInt(...) || 14` treated a legitimately-typed `0` for chat hearing range as falsy and silently overrode it to 14); `PurseClubArea.ts` (computed `clubLevel` inline from `sessionDataManager` with `isVIP` checked *before* `hasClub`, inverting AS3's priority — a VIP flag with 0 club days/periods showed the VIP icon instead of the "no club" state; fixed by porting the real `HabboInventory.clubLevel` getter, which didn't exist in the TS port at all, and pointing this class at it); `FurniView.ts` (invalid/reformatted trade quantity input reset the count to a literal `0` and submitted a 0-item trade offer instead of clamping to `Math.max(1, ...)` like the AS3 source); `MeMenuSoundSettingsSlider.ts` and `RoomCreateViewCtrl.ts::centerContent()` (both used `Math.round`/kept fractional pixel offsets where AS3's `int`-typed setters truncate toward zero, not round).
- 🐛 **Known gap, not yet fixed**: `IlluminaBorderWidget.ts::refresh()` is an empty stub (`// TODO: Border drawing stub`) and none of its 12 property setters (padding, side padding, child margin, 6 child slots, landing view mode, border style) call it. AS3's `refresh()` does the entire 9-slice border draw plus content/side-padding and top/bottom child positioning math (`sources/win63_version/.../IlluminaBorderWidget.as::refresh()` — note: the win63 copy is decompiler-corrupted here, `switch(0 % 3)` and a dropped top/bottom branch; use `sources/PRODUCTION-201601012205-226667486/.../IlluminaBorderWidget.as` as the clean reference). This widget's entire layout system is currently inert.
- 🐛 **Second audit batch: 5 more AS3-divergence bugs found and fixed, `habbo/session` + `habbo/communication` (encryption/login/parser) audited**: `RoomSession.ts::set roomControllerLevel()` silently ignored an out-of-range value instead of resetting to `ROOM_CONTROL_LEVEL_NONE` (0) like AS3 — an invalid/corrupt controller-level from the server left the user at their previous elevated permission level instead of being demoted; `FloorHeightMapMessageParser.ts` was missing the `cameraInitX`/`cameraInitY`/`cameraInitZ` wire fields entirely (AS3 reads them right after `areaHideData`; zero references anywhere in the engine before this fix); `HeightMapMessageParser.ts` computed `_height = total / this._width` without truncation, diverging from AS3's `int`-typed assignment for malformed/non-exact-multiple payloads; `HabboLocalizationManager.ts` had three issues — `getExternalVariablesUrl()`/`getExternalVariablesHash()` read dead fields that were never assigned anywhere (now delegate to `getGameDataResources()`), `requestLocalizationInit()` had an invented `else` branch calling `loadExternalTexts()` that matches neither win63 nor PRODUCTION-201601012205-226667486 AS3 (confirmed `gamedata.hashes.url` is always set via `common_configuration_txt.txt`, so the branch was dead in practice — removed), and a failed load only logged an error instead of calling `Core.crash()` like AS3, so anything awaiting `'complete'` would hang silently instead of surfacing a fatal error. Also wired the previously-inert `flags:uint` constructor parameter (`HabboConfigurationFlags.SKIP_EXTERNAL_VARIABLES`/`SKIP_LOCALIZATIONS`) through `Component` → `CoreLocalizationManager` → `HabboLocalizationManager`/`HabboConfigurationManager`, including `HabboConfigurationManager.resetAll()`'s previously-missing final branch (immediate unlock+complete vs. eager `initConfigurationDownload()`) — behaviorally inert today since nothing passes non-default flags yet, but now matches the full AS3 member signature. `HabboConfigurationManager.as::parseArguments()` (flashvars/query-string config overrides from `(context as ICore).arguments`, `_`→`.` key normalization, then `clearArguments()`) was unported entirely — now implemented and wired into `resetAll()` at the correct call site; behaviorally inert today since nothing currently populates `ICore.arguments`, but no longer a silent gap. `communication/encryption` (ArcFour, DiffieHellman) and `communication/login` audited clean — no divergence (note: win63's `ArcFour.as` KSA swap is itself decompiler-corrupted, `sbox[j]=0` instead of completing the swap; the TS port already has the correct swap, don't "fix" it to match win63). `communication/messages/parser` loop-bearing files (79 of 339) partially audited, most of `communication/messages/incoming`/`outgoing` (600+ simple field composers) and 58 of the 79 loop-bearing parsers not yet covered — candidates for a follow-up pass.
- 🐛 **Third audit batch: 10 more AS3-divergence bugs found and fixed in `habbo/communication/messages/{incoming,outgoing}/{room,inventory}`** (targeted audit of the actual wire-format composers/parsers, not just the higher-level model/UI code):
  - `RoomDimmerChangeStateComposer.ts`/`RoomDimmerGetPresetsComposer.ts` sent an empty array — AS3 both take a single `objectId:int` (the dimmer furni's item id, confirmed via `FurnitureDimmerWidgetHandler.as` call sites) and send `[objectId]`. `RoomDimmerSavePresetComposer.ts` was missing 2 of AS3's 7 wire fields (a hardcoded `false` and the `objectId`). All three now take the id and match AS3's array shape; wired through `RoomSession.ts`'s previously-unused `_itemId` params. Zero live impact today (the dimmer UI widget itself isn't ported yet), but was blocking that port from ever working.
  - `MountPetComposer.ts`/`DismountPetComposer.ts` — AS3 has exactly ONE composer, `MountPetMessageComposer(petId, mount:Boolean)`; the TS port invented a split into two composers that both dropped the boolean entirely, relying on an assumption ("server toggles state") not in AS3. Merged back into one `MountPetComposer(petId, mount)`, deleted the invented `DismountPetComposer`.
  - `BanUserWithDurationMessageComposer.ts` — wrong field order (`[userId, banType, roomId]` vs AS3's `[userId, roomId, banType]`) **and** wrong type (`banType` retyped as a number with an invented 1/2/0 mapping; AS3 sends the literal ban-duration string). `RoomSession.banUserWithDuration()`'s hand-rolled string→number switch was removed; the AS3 string constant now flows straight through.
  - `TradingItemListMessageParser.ts` reused `FurniListItemParser` (the regular inventory item format) to parse trade-offer items — AS3 uses a completely different class (`class_3066`: adds `isGroupable` before `stuffData`, replaces the tradeable/sellable/rent-period flags with `creationDay`/`creationMonth`/`creationYear`). Created `TradingFurniItemParser.ts` matching `class_3066` field-for-field; every traded item was being misparsed from the first field onward.
  - `PetInventoryMessageParser.ts` was densely wrong: read `breedId`'s wire slot as a loop count, undercounted the custom-parts loop by 3x (AS3 reads 3 ints per custom part, TS read 1), invented two fields that don't exist on the wire (`hasCustomLayerPart`, `petRarity`), and hardcoded `level: 1` with a comment claiming "level comes in a separate message" — false, AS3 reads it as the very next field. Rewritten to match `class_2838`/`class_2486` exactly; the parser cursor was desynced from the first pet entry onward.
  - `BadgesMessageParser.ts` (inventory) never read `totalFragments`/`fragmentNo`, and split parsing into two invented phases (a badge-id-only loop, then a separate slotId+badgeId loop) instead of AS3's single `count` + `(slotId, badgeId)` loop. Rewritten to match `BadgesEventParser.as`; cross-checked against the already-correct `HabboUserBadgesMessageParser.ts` (users module), which has the right shape and confirmed this was a regression specific to the inventory-badges file.
  - `FurniListAddOrUpdateMessageParser.ts` — **live bug**: parsed exactly one item with a comment claiming "AS3 reads 1 item, but message may contain more" (backwards — AS3 reads a `count` then loops `count` times). `HabboInventory.onFurniListAddOrUpdate()` already correctly loops over `parser.items`, so this was silently dropping/corrupting every item after the first whenever the server batched more than one add/update in a single message.
  - `FurniListRemoveMultipleEventParser.as` (bulk furni removal, message ID 149) had no TS port at all — zero references anywhere in the engine. Fully ported: `FurniListRemoveMultipleMessageParser.ts`, `FurniListRemoveMultipleMessageEvent.ts`, registered in `HabboMessages.ts`, and wired into `HabboInventory.ts::onFurniListRemoveMultiple()` calling the new `FurniModel.removeFurnis()` (added to `FurniModel`/`IFurniModel`, matching AS3's `removeFurnis()`).
  - Audited clean, no divergence: `communication/messages/{incoming,outgoing}/navigator` + `newnavigator` (96 files — one dead-code constructor overload noted, not a bug), all `incoming/room` wrapper files (47), `outgoing/room/action`/`avatar`/`pet`(except Mount/Dismount)/`settings`/most of `furniture`(except the 3 RoomDimmer bugs), `outgoing/room/engine` (MoveAvatar/MoveObject/PlaceObject/PickupObject/GetFurnitureAliases).
  - Not yet covered (follow-up candidates): `outgoing/room/chat`/`session` (8 files), most of `incoming`/`outgoing` `inventory`/`users` beyond the item-list parsers above (~90 files, `BlockListMessageParser`/`IgnoredUsersMessageParser` flagged as the likely next risk), and `help`/`notifications`/`friendlist`/`moderation`/`quest`/`handshake`/`catalog`/etc. incoming/outgoing directories (untouched this pass).
- ✅ **ClubCenter ported** (`habbo/catalog/clubcenter/`): `HabboClubCenter` (manager, `ILinkEventTracker` on `habboUI/` — this is what makes `HabboCatalog.openClubCenter()`'s pre-existing `habboUI/open/hccenter` link actually do something), `ClubCenterView` (status/badge/gift-count popup), `ClubSpecialInfoBubbleView` (payday breakdown callout), `util/ClubStatus.ts`, `util/BadgeResolver.ts`. New message infra it needed: `ClubGiftInfoEvent`/`ClubGiftInfoEventParser` (+ `ClubOfferData`/`ClubOfferProductData`/`ClubGiftEligibilityData`) and `GetClubGiftMessageComposer`, IDs 3771/1604 — none of this existed before. Wired into `VortexMain.ts::prepareCore()` under `IID_HabboClubCenter` (the IID stub already existed, typed `unknown`, now typed properly).
  - Two intentional adaptations, not gaps: (1) `hc_badge` in `club_center.json` was switched from a raw `bitmap` tag (AS3's approach: `SessionDataManager.requestBadgeImage()` + a `BIRE_BADGE_IMAGE_READY` retry event) to `widget_type: badge_image`, reusing the already-working `BadgeImageWidget` instead of re-deriving that retry dance. (2) `IRoomPreviewerWidget.showPreview()` was never actually implemented before this (stored a URL, rendered nothing, zero callers) — this port is its first real caller; the avatar preview Texture is converted to a data URL via a plain 2D canvas (`AvatarImage.getCroppedImage()` is backed directly by an `OffscreenCanvas`, no PixiJS renderer/GPU readback needed).
  - Known gap, tagged `TODO(AS3)` in `HabboClubCenter.ts::initComponent()`: the "earn credits via video" offer button (`btn_earn`) has no real backing system — `IOfferCenter`/`IOfferExtension` don't exist anywhere (`catalog/offers/` is empty) and `IHabboCatalog.getOfferCenter()`/`getHabboClubOffers()` are stubs. AS3 itself gates this button behind `offers.enabled`+`offers.habboclub.enabled` config flags, so it stays hidden exactly as it would with those flags off — not an invented workaround, just inert until the offer-center system is ported.
- ✅ **`habbo/freeflowchat` is now the live, wired-in chat-bubble renderer** (supersedes the "standalone, unwired" state described in earlier snapshots) — ported across 5 phases per a scoped "Plan 1: foundations" (full asset pipeline + bubble rendering + style selector + simple non-colliding placement), followed by a "Plan 2" pass (real `ChatFlowStage` gravity/collision physics + avatar face-crop) once Plan 1 was verified live against a real server:
  - **Asset pipeline** (`tools/import-chatstyles.mjs`, new): hash-preserving extraction of the 89-style `chatstyles_xml` catalog plus each style's `regpoints`/bitmap assets (background, pointer, emblem, emblem-multiline, icon, selector-preview, color) from `WIN63-202607011411-782849652`. `import-crypted-images.mjs` couldn't be reused as-is — its `resolveRawFileName()` strips the `$<hash>` suffix off embed names, which would've aliased all 89 different `style_<id>_chat_bubble_base` embeds onto a single raw file; the new tool resolves by the full hash-bearing value instead. Runtime glue: `App.ts::registerChatStyleImageAssets()` registers each PNG as a raw `ImageBitmap` (not the standard `image/png`→`BitmapDataAsset`/PixiJS-`Texture` pipeline — `ChatStyle.getNewBackgroundSprite()` needs a real `ImageBitmap` for `OffscreenCanvas.drawImage()`), while `VortexMain.ts::registerChatStyleTextAssets()` registers the XML catalog and `regpoints` text synchronously during `prepareCore()`.
  - **`ChatStyleLibrary`**: TS-only divergence from AS3 (documented in-code) — this port's per-style bitmaps register into the asset library *asynchronously* (after `Vortex.bootstrap()` resolves), unlike AS3's synchronously-embedded assets, so styles are built lazily on first `getStyle()`/`getStyleIds()` access instead of eagerly in the constructor (an eager-build attempt during development raced the async image registration and silently built every style with an `undefined` background — caught during manual verification, see the bug list below).
  - **`ChatBubble`/`PooledChatBubble`** (`viewer/visualization/`): full AS3 rendering ported — 9-slice tinted background, pointer bitmap tracked to the speaker's screen X, emblem, HTML-markup-aware text (`ChatTextLayout.ts`'s run-based canvas layout, since this port has no PixiJS `HTMLText` equivalent anywhere in the codebase; `ChatMarkup.ts` ports the AS3 bracket-tag markup), overflow clip mask, 150ms linear move tween. `ChatBubble` wraps an internal `PooledChatBubble` instance rather than duplicating AS3's near-identical composition logic across both classes.
  - **`ChatFlowViewer`/`ChatFlowStage`/`ChatViewController`** (`viewer/`, `viewer/simulation/`): initially shipped as a simple, non-colliding placeholder (bubbles spawn anchored above the speaker and scroll upward at a constant rate, no collision avoidance) — since replaced by the real AS3 physics engine, see the "Plan 2" bullet below.
  - **`ChatStyleSelector`** (`habbo/ui/widget/chatinput/styleselector/`, new directory): grid + font-size popup wired into `RoomChatInputView`'s "styles" button, with id-range/`isHcOnly`/`isAmbassadorOnly`/`isStaffOverrideable`/purchasable/NFT-ownership filtering. Two deliberate deviations from upstream AS3 bugs (flagged in-code): `_selected`/`_styleRequiresUpdate` are per-instance instead of AS3's class-`static` fields, and `dispose()` fully clears `_entries` instead of AS3's `while(length>1)pop()` (which leaves one stray entry in every source tree checked).
  - **`RoomUI`/`RoomDesktop`/`ChatWidgetHandler`**: `freeFlowChat.displayObject` now mounts into the room's chat container (`RoomDesktopLayoutManager.getChatContainer()`, retyped from `IWindowContainer` to `IDisplayObjectWrapper` to match the actual layout element), and the old `RoomChatWidget` path is disabled via an `if(container.freeFlowChat) return;` guard in `ChatWidgetHandler`, matching primary/2026 source exactly (no user-preference toggle — that was flash/win63-only and was dropped there).
  - **Thirteen runtime-only bugs found and fixed** (four during manual verification, nine more reported by the user across several follow-up rounds of live testing against a real server — all compiled and linted clean; all broke silently at runtime, none caught by `tsc`):
    1. `VortexMain.ts::prepareCore()` constructed `HabboFreeFlowChat` without passing the asset library (`new HabboFreeFlowChat(ctx)` — every sibling component, e.g. `RoomEngine`/`HabboCatalog`/`RoomUI`, passes `this._assets` as a constructor arg; this one didn't), so `chatFlow.assets` was always `null` and `ChatBubbleFactory` never built a `ChatStyleLibrary` at all.
    2. `IID_HabboFreeFlowChat` was never registered — `ctx.attachComponent(this._freeFlowChat, [])` used an empty interface array, so the `ComponentDependency(IID_HabboFreeFlowChat, ...)` in `RoomUI`/`RoomSessionManager`/`HabboWindowManager` never resolved and `RoomUI._freeFlowChat` stayed `null` forever, silently breaking all of the wiring above despite a clean build.
    3. `PooledChatBubble.update()` only set `visible = true` inside the branch that fires when a `moveTo()`-driven tween completes; AS3's real condition (`PooledChatBubble.as::update()`) is unconditional — `visible` flips true once total elapsed time since `recreate()` exceeds 150ms, regardless of movement state. Since `ChatFlowViewer.insertBubble()` always spawns via `warpTo()` (Plan 1 has no approach animation), which never sets the tween-start marker the port's version depended on, every bubble was permanently invisible. Fixed to match AS3's unconditional elapsed-time check.
    4. `ChatTextLayout.ts::layoutChatText()` (pure text-layout bug, not wiring): consecutive words within the same styled run rendered with no space between them (`"Hello world"` → `"Helloworld"`) — the word-wrap loop measured a whitespace segment's width for line-break positioning but never wrote the space character into any placed run's `text`, and the same-run merge path (`previous.text + word`) then joined the two words directly. Fixed by appending the space onto the preceding placed run before the next word can merge into it.
    5. **`RoomChatInputView.ts::createWindow()` broke chat typing entirely on a real server** (`custom.chat.styles.enabled`-gated code path the headless-boot check never reached, since it needs a live room, not just the login screen). The port called `createOrUpdateChatStylesView()` *before* attaching `chat_input`'s own `WindowMouseEvent.DOWN`/`WindowKeyboardEvent.KEY_DOWN`/`WE_CHANGE` listeners; real AS3 (`RoomChatInputView.as::createWindow()`, lines 300-345) does the opposite — `_window.procedure` is set immediately after building the window, the input's own listeners are attached next, and `createOrUpdateChatStylesView()` runs dead last. The style-selector construction (see bug 6) was throwing synchronously and aborting `createWindow()` *before* the input listeners got attached, so no keystroke ever reached the text field. Reordered to match AS3 exactly; typing no longer depends on the chat-style feature succeeding.
    6. **`ItemGridController`'s `resize_on_item_update` auto-grow fights `ChatStyleSelector.gridColumns`'s width cap, so the style grid never wraps** — reported by the user as "it's a grid but renders as one giant horizontal strip" (and, per bug 7, contributed to that strip becoming wide enough to interfere with normal chat input, though it turned out not to be the actual cause of that). Traced with a stack-trace-logging harness driven live against the actual running app (no server needed for this one — reproduced by constructing the same `styleselector_menu_new_xml`/`chatinput_chatstyle_template_xml` windows the real code uses): `itemgrid`'s layout node carries `resize_on_item_update="true"` (genuine AS3 layout data). Every `addGridItem()` call that creates a new column runs `ItemListController.addListItem()` on the grid's own outer list, which grows the grid's internal `_container` by one cell-width; because `_container` has AS3's `EXPAND_TO_ACCOMMODATE_CHILDREN`-equivalent param bit (`0x400000`) armed, that growth immediately propagates back up to the grid's own `.width` (`WindowController.ts`'s `WE_RESIZED` handler: `this._parent.width = this._parent.width + (this._width - this._previousRect.width)`). So `ItemGridController.resolveColumnForNextItem()`'s wrap decision (`lastCol.right + item.width <= this._width`) was always comparing against a width that had *just* grown to fit one more column — never failing, so it never wrapped to a new row; confirmed by a stack trace showing the grid's own `setRectangle` being invoked recursively via `_container`'s resize propagation. Fixed by also pinning `grid.limits.maxWidth` to the same value `gridColumns` computes (`ChatStyleSelector.ts`) — the pre-existing `IWindow.limits`/`IRectLimiter` rect-clamp (`setRectangle()` already clamps against `_rectLimits.maxWidth` before applying any resize) now caps the auto-grow at the intended column budget without touching `resize_on_item_update` itself or any other `ItemGridController` consumer. Verified with 24 real chat styles: was one ~1300px-wide single row, now wraps correctly into a 6-column × 4-row grid. Not yet investigated: whether other `ItemGridController` users elsewhere in the codebase that combine `resize_on_item_update="true"` with a manually-assigned fixed `.width` hit the same defect (this fix only pins `maxWidth` for the chat-style selector's own grid instance). A separate, still-open gap found during this same investigation: the per-style bitmap assigned to each cloned grid-cell's `bubble_preview` (`IBitmapWrapperWindow.bitmap`) renders as a plain grey placeholder instead of the style's actual artwork — confirmed the assigned `ImageBitmap` data itself is correct and distinct per style (spot-checked all 89), so the gap is somewhere in `WindowComposite.compositeBitmapWrapper()`'s draw path or its per-window raster cache for freshly-cloned template windows specifically, not yet root-caused.
    7. **`App.ts`'s canvas `mousedown` handler never called `event.preventDefault()`, so every click silently lost keyboard focus a moment after being set** — this was the actual reason typing produced nothing after clicking the chat input, and bug 5's reordering fix (needed on its own merits) didn't touch it. `<canvas>` isn't natively focusable; a browser's default action for an unhandled `mousedown` event blurs whatever currently holds focus (or, if the target itself is focusable, focuses that instead) once every JS listener for the event has finished running — and that default action fires *after* this port's own window-system dispatch, which synchronously calls `TextFieldController.focus()` → `HTMLInputElement.focus()` on the chat input's hidden proxy `<input>`. Without `preventDefault()`, the browser's default action ran right after and silently reverted `document.activeElement` back to `<body>` — the drawn caret still appeared (it's driven by this port's own internal focus flag, set successfully before the revert) so focus *looked* like it worked, but no keystroke ever reached the hidden input again. Reproduced and confirmed in a minimal, app-independent Playwright test (a bare `<canvas>` + hidden `<input>`, `.focus()` called from a `mousedown` listener): `document.activeElement` reverted to `<body>`/canvas without `preventDefault()`, stayed on the input with it. Fixed by calling `e.preventDefault()` at the top of `_onMouseDown`. This is a `vortex-client`-only fix (canvas input handling has no AS3 equivalent — Flash's stage focus model doesn't have this DOM-specific quirk) and affects every text field in the app, not just chat, though chat input was the only one exercised enough during testing to surface it.
    8. **`TextFieldController`'s native `keydown`/`keyup` handlers computed a bogus `charCode` for every non-printable key** — `e.key?.charCodeAt(0)` is correct for printable characters (`e.key` *is* the typed character, e.g. `'a'`/`' '`), but for control keys `e.key` is a multi-character name (`'Enter'`, `'Backspace'`, `'Tab'`, ...), so `.charCodeAt(0)` silently returned the first *letter's* code instead of the intended control-character value — `'Enter'.charCodeAt(0)` is 69 (`'E'`), not 13. `RoomChatInputView.onKeyDown()`'s `event.charCode === 13` send-on-Enter check (and the `=== 32`/space check, which happened to work by coincidence since `' '` is itself length-1) therefore never matched Enter, so typing worked (confirmed after bug 7's fix) but pressing Enter did nothing. Added `TextFieldController.resolveCharCode()`: uses `e.key.charCodeAt(0)` when `e.key.length === 1` (printable), falls back to the legacy `e.keyCode` otherwise (still carries the expected values for control keys: Enter=13, Backspace=8, Tab=9, ...). Affects every text field's Enter/Backspace/Tab/Escape handling app-wide, not just chat.
    9. **Chat bubbles never appeared for real messages despite rendering correctly in isolated testing** — `ChatEventHandler.onRoomChat()` (`habbo/freeflowchat/data/`) had `const userLocation = null;` as an explicit, documented `TODO(AS3)` stub ("when `IRoomEngine.getRoomObject` is available") — it already was, this just hadn't been wired up. Every bubble's `PooledChatBubble.userScreenPos` getter early-returns `{x: 0, y: 0}` when `item.userLocation` is `null` (`if(!item.userLocation) return {x: 0, y: 0};`), so `ChatFlowStage.insertBubble()` always anchored every bubble at the room canvas's top-left corner instead of above the speaker — invisible/nonsensical in practice, not just visually off. This gap was invisible to this feature's own manual verification (bugs 1-4 above) because those tests called `PooledChatBubble.warpTo()` directly with hand-picked coordinates, bypassing `ChatFlowStage`'s positioning path entirely — the position pipeline itself was never actually exercised until a real server round-trip. Fixed by porting the real AS3 lookup from `ChatEventHandler.as::onRoomChat()` (previously not read closely enough for this method): `roomEngine.getRoomObject(event.session.roomId, event.userId, 100).getLocation()` (note: AS3 passes `event.userId` directly as the object id here, not `userData.roomObjectId` — different from the same lookup elsewhere in `ChatBubbleFactory`). Also ported the adjacent `sessionDataManager.isBlocked(senderData.webID)` early-return from the same AS3 method, silently dropped in the original port along with the location lookup.
    10. **`ChatFlowStage` never ticked** — found immediately after bug 9 while re-checking the whole per-frame update path: unlike `ChatFlowViewer` (whose constructor calls `chatFlow.registerUpdateReceiver(this, 1)`), the ported `ChatFlowStage` constructor never called `registerUpdateReceiver` at all, so its `update()` (upward scroll + off-screen `readyToRecycle` cleanup) was dead code, never invoked by anything. Confirmed against real AS3 (`ChatFlowStage.as::ChatFlowStage()`, which does call `registerUpdateReceiver(this, 2)` — a *different* priority than `ChatFlowViewer`'s 1, i.e. two independently-registered receivers, not one delegating to the other, matching what `ChatFlowViewer.as::update()` shows: it never calls into `ChatFlowStage` either). Added the missing `registerUpdateReceiver(this, 2)` call (plus the matching `removeUpdateReceiver` in `dispose()`, mirroring `ChatFlowViewer`'s own pattern). Doesn't affect a bubble's initial appearance (that path was already fine), but without it bubbles would never scroll upward or ever get recycled/removed once spawned.
    11. **`HabboFreeFlowChat.getScreenPointFromRoomLocation()`'s earlier port dropped AS3's stage-recentring term, producing wildly off-screen bubble positions** (e.g. x=-534 for a speaker standing at a perfectly normal, on-screen tile). `RoomGeometry.getScreenPoint()` returns a point relative to the isometric camera's own centre, not the canvas's top-left corner — real AS3 (`HabboFreeFlowChat.as::getScreenPointFromRoomLocation()`) recentres it via `(stage.stageWidth * canvasScale / 2) + rawPoint.x * canvasScale + offset.x` (same for Y/stageHeight) before returning. An earlier pass through this method (this session, before the bug was found) deliberately dropped that term, reasoning "`ChatFlowViewer.rootDisplayObject` already shares the room's own coordinate space, so `getScreenPoint()` + `offset` alone (proven correct by the legacy `ChatWidgetHandler.ts`) should apply directly" — wrong on both counts: (a) `ChatWidgetHandler`'s *own* working code recentres too, just differently (`RoomChatWidget.ts:640`: `item.x = x + this._container.width/2 + this._container.x`), missed on the earlier read; (b) bug 12 below shows `rootDisplayObject` isn't even part of the room's coordinate space at all. Root-caused via a live diagnostic harness the user ran in DevTools (patched `ChatFlowStage.insertBubble()`/`ChatEventHandler` in the running app to log every intermediate value for a real chat message) — confirmed `getScreenPoint()` was returning small, sane numbers (e.g. `{x: -64, y: -28}` for a speaker at room tile `(3, 5, 0)`) that were never being recentred onto the visible viewport. Restored the full AS3 formula, using `window.innerWidth`/`innerHeight` as the `stage.stageWidth`/`stageHeight` stand-in (same convention `PooledChatBubble.ts` already uses elsewhere in this feature, since this port has one shared PixiJS canvas rather than AS3's per-room Flash sub-stages).
    12. **`ChatFlowViewer.rootDisplayObject` (the container every chat bubble lives in) was never actually added to the PixiJS stage** — the single biggest gap, found via a second live diagnostic (walking `rootDisplayObject`'s parent chain up to `application.stage`, and dumping `stage.children`): the chain terminated one level short of `stage`, and `stage.children` had exactly one entry, the room's own `RoomRenderingCanvas` — the bubble container was parented only within the window system's own bookkeeping tree (built for `WindowComposite`'s Canvas2D compositing pass, a *separate* rendering path from the WebGL/PixiJS stage; see `DisplayObjectWrapperController.ts`). `RoomUI.ts`'s existing `getChatContainer()?.setDisplayObject(...)` call (Phase 2) only registers the reference so `WindowComposite` can punch a transparent hole in its Canvas2D UI layer at that screen rect — a hole reveals whatever's *already rendering* on the PixiJS stage underneath it, and since `rootDisplayObject` was never a stage descendant, there was nothing there to reveal. No AS3 equivalent (Flash has one unified display tree, no Canvas2D/WebGL split), so this is TS-port-only infrastructure that was simply never finished: added `IRoomEngine.addStageChild()`/`removeStageChild()` (mounts/unmounts a display object directly on the PixiJS stage, above every room rendering canvas already added — the same `_pixiStage` reference `RoomRenderingCanvas` mounting already uses) and call it from `HabboFreeFlowChat.roomEntered()`/`roomLeft()`.
    13. **`ChatFlowStage`'s upward scroll used an invented continuous-creep model instead of AS3's real discrete-jump timer, so simultaneous bubbles visually piled up without appearing to move** — once bugs 9-12 above got bubbles actually appearing on screen, the user reported them "stacking" instead of scrolling away. The Plan-1 placeholder computed a `pixels/ms` rate (`MOVE_UP_AMOUNT_PIXELS / 10000`) and applied a tiny fractional move every frame — real AS3 (`ChatFlowStage.as::update()`) instead accumulates elapsed time and, once `MOVE_UP_TIMER_DEFAULT` (10000ms — the only value this port ever sees, since `roomChatSettings.scrollSpeed`, which would otherwise pick 3000/6000/12000ms, is a documented always-null `TODO(AS3)` stub) has passed, jumps every bubble up by the full `MOVE_UP_AMOUNT_PIXELS` (19px) at once via `scrollUp()`, then resets the timer. At ~0.0019px/frame the old continuous version was movement well below the threshold of human perception, so bubbles read as permanently stuck even though they were technically inching upward. Rewrote `update()` to match the real timer-accumulate-then-jump model (still without AS3's gravity/collision-avoidance physics, which stays deferred to "Plan 2" per this section's header) — `bubble.moveTo()`'s existing 150ms tween smooths each 19px jump into a visible but non-jarring motion, matching what a real Habbo client shows.
  - Verified end-to-end via a headless-Chromium harness driving the actual running app (no live game server available for a full login→room→chat test): all 89 style-preview thumbnails render as real, visually distinct images, and a fully composited bubble (9-slice background + pointer + bold username + italic multi-line word-wrapped message body) renders correctly across multiple different styles.
- ✅ **"Plan 2" — real `ChatFlowStage` physics + avatar face-crop** — after Plan 1 shipped, the user reported (live, against a real server) that simultaneous speakers' bubbles overlapped messily instead of stacking like real Habbo, and that bubbles showed no avatar face; both were the two gaps explicitly deferred above, and were ported in full rather than patched around:
  - **`ChatBubbleSimulationEntity`/`ChatBubbleSimulationWithLimitedWideRect`/`ChatBubbleCollisionEvent`/`ChatFlowGravity`** (new, `viewer/simulation/`): full port of AS3's per-bubble physics state (`visualRect`/`wideRect` AABBs, horizontal/vertical impulse accumulation with negative-feedback smoothing on `x`), the pairwise collision wrapper, and the gravity/attraction helper. `ChatFlowGravity`'s real name was only recoverable from the **tertiary** source tree (`PRODUCTION-201601012205-226667486`) — obfuscated as `_SafeCls_2911`/`"_-oC"` in the primary tree and left as the generic, still-unrecovered `class_3255` in the secondary tree, so per project rules on recovering real identifiers the tertiary tree was used instead of inventing a name.
  - **`ChatFlowStage`** (`viewer/simulation/`, rewritten): replaced the Plan-1 placeholder with the real algorithm — `insertBubble()` relaxes a new bubble toward its speaker's existing bubbles via up to 10 rounds of `ChatFlowGravity` pull before clamping it back within 15px of the speaker; every `update()` tick runs `simulate()`, up to 20 rounds of pairwise AABB intersection + impulse resolution (sideways nudge if bubbles overlap ≤15px horizontally, otherwise a vertical push separating whichever bubble is older/lower); `scrollUp()` still jumps every bubble `MOVE_UP_AMOUNT_PIXELS` (19px) every scroll interval as before, now followed by a gravity re-centring pass and a `simulate()` call. Line-by-line mode (spacer bubbles, vertical-only resolution) is ported for fidelity and reachable once a real account-preferences message sets `chatMode=1` (see bug 14 below) — defaults to free-flow (mode 0) until then. `PooledChatBubble.scrolledUserPositionX` (new getter) was needed to support the entities' per-frame speaker-tracking (`syncToUserScreenPosition()`); confirmed several of the class's declared constants (`MINIMUM_COLLIDER_WIDTH`, `ChatBubbleSimulationWithLimitedWideRect.WIDERECT_WIDTH`, `ChatFlowGravity`'s three `INPUT_GRAVITY_*` constants) are dead in the AS3 source itself — declared but every call site hardcodes the matching literal instead of referencing them — and ported that exact literal-vs-symbolic split rather than "cleaning it up".
  - **`ChatBubbleFactory.getUserImage()`** (`viewer/`, implemented — no longer a stub): builds the bubble's avatar head-crop via `avatarRenderManager.createAvatarImage(figureString, zoom.enabled ? 'h' : 'sh', ...)` + the already-fully-ported `HabboFaceFocuser.focusUserFace()` (turned out to need only wiring, not new infrastructure — both `HabboFaceFocuser` and a `Texture`→`ImageBitmap` path already existed from unrelated earlier work, contrary to the stale "neither exists yet" note this replaces). Required exposing `avatarRenderManager` and `getBoolean()` on `IHabboFreeFlowChat` (mirroring the existing `HabboToolbar` DI pattern) since `ChatBubbleFactory` only ever holds the interface, not the concrete `HabboFreeFlowChat`.
  - **Bug 15 (found 2026-07-11, same live user report as bug 14: face crop still not rendering after the wiring above): `AvatarImage.getImage()`/`getCroppedImage()` silently ignored their own `scale` parameter — a genuine, pre-existing gap in `habbo/avatar/`, unrelated to freeflowchat, just never exercised by any caller with `scale !== 1` before `HabboFaceFocuser` became reachable.** A three-round live diagnostic (console logging → per-field sprite/texture inspection → visually rendering the actual bitmaps to an on-page canvas overlay, since the numeric checks all came back valid) showed the full avatar render was correct but the face crop returned a fully transparent 25×25 bitmap — the crop rectangle was landing in empty margin left of the head, not on it. Root cause, confirmed by reading `AvatarImage.as::getImage()` line-by-line: real AS3 calls `class_2495.resampleBitmapData(bitmap, scale)` on the composited canvas whenever `scale != 1` *before* handing it back — `HabboFaceFocuser.focusUserFace()`'s `X_OFFSETS`/`Y_OFFSETS` crop coordinates (already correctly ported, confirmed byte-for-byte against AS3) are calibrated assuming that resampling already happened, i.e. that a `scale=0.5` call returns a canvas *half the pixel size* of an unscaled one. This port's `getImage()` (and the sibling `getCroppedImage()`, same bug) never applied any resampling at all, so `zoom.enabled=true` sessions (`"h"`/large avatar scale + 0.5x icon-crop multiplier) got back a full-size, unshrunk canvas and cropped the wrong region — invisible/transparent result, not a crash, so nothing else caught it. Ported the real resample algorithm as `AvatarImage.resampleCanvas()`/`resizeCanvas()` (progressive halving below 0.5x scale for quality, matching `resampleBitmapData()`/`resizeBitmapData()` exactly) and wired it into both methods, including AS3's specific quirk of caching the *unscaled* bitmap and only resampling the returned copy (so a later same-pose call at a different scale still recomputes correctly). The containing AS3 class's real name is unrecoverable from any of the three source trees (obfuscated `_SafeCls_2871` in the primary tree, generic decompiler name `class_2495` in the secondary tree, absent from the tertiary tree) — the method names themselves are real/legible, only the wrapper class isn't, so `resampleCanvas`/`resizeCanvas` are private statics on `AvatarImage` rather than a wrongly-named standalone class. Independently corroborated by ESLint: `pnpm lint` had *already* been flagging `scale` as an unused parameter on both methods before this fix (a real, live lint error, not just a stylistic warning) — confirms the gap was there prior to this session, not something introduced by the chat-bubble wiring.
  - **`getPetImage()` stays a stub, now for a precisely-scoped reason** (not the vague placeholder it replaces): `IRoomEngine.getPetImage()` doesn't exist and there is no pet-avatar sprite-composition code anywhere in `habbo/room/` — unlike the user-avatar case, this isn't a wiring gap but a missing subsystem comparable in scope to `AvatarRenderManager` itself, out of scope for this pass; documented `TODO(AS3)` in-code with the exact blocking dependency chain, cross-referenced against the identical gap already noted in `ChatWidgetHandler.ts`.
  - **Bug 14 (found 2026-07-11, live user report: "remonte pas assez vite" - scrolls up noticeably too slowly): `roomChatSettings` was permanently `null`, capping the scroll interval at `ChatFlowStage`'s 10000ms fallback instead of AS3's real ~6000ms default.** Root-caused by reading `HabboFreeFlowChat.as`'s actual constructor: it calls `refreshEffectiveChatSettings()` immediately (`_chatMode=0, _chatBubbleWidth=1, scrollSpeed=1, floodSensitivity=1` field defaults), so AS3's `roomChatSettings` is *never* null - `ChatFlowStage.refreshSettings()` always finds it non-null and resolves `scrollSpeed=1` to the 6000ms tier from the very first frame, well before any server message arrives. This port's `HabboFreeFlowChat` never did that self-initialization, leaving `roomChatSettings` null forever and `ChatFlowStage` stuck on its documented-as-unreachable 10000ms fallback - a real, measurable (67% slower) behavioral gap, not just a cosmetic one. Fixed: `HabboFreeFlowChat`'s constructor now calls a new `refreshEffectiveChatSettings()` (matching AS3's own method + defaults exactly) to self-initialize `roomChatSettings`; also wired the real `onAccountPreferences()` handler (`AccountPreferencesEvent`, message 2082) so a genuine server preference update still overrides the default afterward. This surfaced a second, independent bug in the process: `AccountPreferencesParser.ts` stopped reading 11 fields early (after `preferedChatStyle`), silently dropping `chatMode`/`chatBubbleWidth`/`chatScrollSpeed`/`chatSizePreference` (plus `wiredMenuButton`/`wiredInspectButton`/`playTestMode`/`wiredWhisperDisabled`/`showAllNotifications`/`wiredUiStyle`) even though the real AS3 parser (recovered from `src/unknowns/_SafePkg_1927/_SafeCls_1926.as` - genuinely part of the client despite the path, directly imported by `HabboFreeFlowChat.as`/`SessionDataManager.as`, unlike the rest of `src/unknowns/`) reads all of them, the last six behind `bytesAvailable > 0` guards for older/shorter server builds. Rewrote the parser to match field-for-field. `onRoomChatSettings()`/`onGuestRoomData()` (AS3's two other `refreshEffectiveChatSettings()` call sites, both only ever touching `floodSensitivity`) are deliberately not wired - `floodSensitivity` isn't exposed on `IRoomChatSettings` and nothing in this port reads it yet, documented `TODO(AS3)` in both `HabboFreeFlowChat.ts` and `IHabboFreeFlowChat.ts`.
  - **Deferred, still `TODO(AS3)`**: the chat-history drag-down tray (`ChatHistoryTray`/`ChatHistoryScrollView` — a distinct feature AS3 bundles in the same container; its toggle button stays a no-op) and pet-avatar chat-bubble faces (`getPetImage()`, above).
  - **Bug 16 (found 2026-07-20, live user report: picking a new bubble style works for the session but never sticks across relogin): `preferedChatStyle`/`chatFontSizeMode`'s setters never persisted the choice server-side, only the composer to do so was missing.** `ChatInputWidgetHandler.ts` already resolves and remembers a style change in-memory for the current session (`freeFlowChat.preferedChatStyle = chatMessage.styleId`, confirmed already wired), and `onAccountPreferences()` already reads `parser.preferedChatStyle` back on login — the two setters just had their real AS3 send commented out behind a `TODO: Send SetChatStylePreferenceComposer when composer is implemented`. Recovered the composer from the primary source (`_SafePkg_2091/_SafeCls_2257.as`, header 2634 in `_SafeCls_2046.as`) — a 2-int `(preferedChatStyle, chatFontSizeMode)` composer, the 2026-era 2-arg evolution of the older 1-arg form the stale comment referenced — added `SetChatStylePreferenceComposer.ts` (`outgoing/preferences/`) and wired both setters to send it with the current value of both fields, matching AS3's `set preferedChatStyle()`/`set chatFontSizeMode()` exactly (both setters send the same composer). `isDisabledInPreferences`'s separate `SetChatPreferencesMessageComposer` gap is unrelated and stays deferred.
- ✅ **Custom Volter glyph rasterizer for chat bubbles** (`core/text/rasterizer/`, new — `ttfOutline.ts`/`outlineFlatten.ts`/`distanceRaster.ts`/`glyphRasterizer.ts`/`flashTextDraw.ts`/`FontOutlineRegistry.ts`): `ChatTextLayout.ts`'s `layoutChatText()`/`drawChatText()` measured and drew chat text via the browser's native `ctx.font`/`ctx.measureText()`/`ctx.fillText()`, which (a) never reproduced Flash's actual text rasterizer and (b) was silently falling back to a generic system font — `App.ts::loadWebFonts()` registers the `FontFace` under family `"Volter (Goldfish)"`, but `ChatStyleLibrary.as`'s real default (`"Volter"`, no suffix — verified byte-for-byte at `ChatStyleLibrary.as:114` in the primary `WIN63-202607011411-782849652` source, confirmed the current AS3-authoritative build) never matched it, so the CSS font-family lookup silently skipped straight to `Arial, sans-serif`. Not an AS3-portable fix (Flash's embedded-font rasterization is native Player engine code, no AS3 source exists for it) — instead, a from-scratch TrueType outline parser + polygon rasterizer reads the project's existing bundled `Volter.ttf`/`Volter Bold.ttf` directly and draws real glyph shapes via `putImageData()`, replacing both measurement and drawing so they can't disagree with each other the way two independent native-font calls could. Deliberately scoped to Volter/`anti-alias-type: normal` only (matches TextStyleManager's real Habbo CSS for every `chat_*` style) — Ubuntu/`anti-alias-type: advanced` text in `WindowComposite.ts` (window captions, buttons, dialogs) still renders via native `ctx.font` and is unaffected by this pass. Falls back to the native path automatically if `FontOutlineRegistry` hasn't registered Volter yet. Researched (read-only, never installed) `iSetht/truffle-text` — a same-day-published, unaudited npm package claiming pixel-perfect Habbo text via ~3MB of reverse-engineered Adobe-AIR calibration data — for its general rasterization *technique* (grid-fit hinting + signed-distance-field density curves); deliberately did not depend on the package, its calibration blobs, its bundled fonts, or its per-glyph magic-number correction tables, since none of that is independently verifiable. Result is a reasoned approximation of Flash's glyph rendering, not a pixel-perfect one — full grid-fit/CSM density-curve rendering for Ubuntu's `advanced` AA styles (`WindowComposite.ts`) is unimplemented follow-up work.
- 🐛 **Full-repo lint cleanup (2026-07-19), surfaced by an unrelated rename.** Renaming `helium-engine`/`helium-client` → `vortex-engine`/`vortex-client` made `git mv` mark every file in both packages as renamed, which made the pre-commit hook's `lint-staged` glob match the entire codebase instead of just genuinely-changed lines — running `eslint` repo-wide for the first time via that gate and surfacing 347 pre-existing errors, none related to the rename itself. Fixing them found several real gaps, not just style:
  - **`GameClickTarget.ts`'s invisible game-interaction hit target never actually rendered anything** — AS3's `update()` lazily creates `new BitmapData(WIDTH, HEIGHT, true, 0)` (a transparent 46×60 placeholder) and assigns it as the sprite's `asset` before setting `alphaTolerance = MATCH_ALL_PIXELS`; the port set the offsets/tolerance but never created or assigned any texture, so `IRoomObjectSprite.texture` (which also drives `width`/`height`, both read-only) stayed `null` and the click target had no hit-testable bounds at all. Fixed by lazily building an equivalent transparent `Texture` via `OffscreenCanvas` + `Texture.from()`, matching the existing `BlankStyle.ts`/`PooledChatBubble.ts` idiom for placeholder textures in this port.
  - **`FurnitureRoomBrandingVisualization.ts` computed `shouldScale`/`effectiveSize` (whether/what to downscale a branded billboard image at icon zoom) and then never used either** — AS3's `checkAndCreateImageForCurrentState()` halves the source bitmap via a 0.5-scale `Matrix` draw when rendering at icon size (32) unless the image URL contains `"noscale"`, and uses the *resulting* (possibly-halved) bitmap's own dimensions for the flip-state offsets; the port always used the full-size texture and the original (never-downscaled) dimensions for offsets. Fixed by porting the downscale via `OffscreenCanvas`+`drawImage` (reading the source `Texture`'s underlying `CanvasImageSource` the same way `ExtendedSprite.ts` already does) and computing offsets from the effective post-scale dimensions, matching AS3's `_loc2_.width`/`_loc2_.height`.
  - **`Exception.ts` silently dropped its own `id` constructor parameter** — AS3's native `Error(message, id)` exposes `id` as `error.errorID`, which real AS3 code reads back out when reporting a caught exception's category (`CoreComponentContext.as`, multiple `error(..., e.errorID, e)` call sites); a JS `Error` has no such slot, and this port's `Exception` subclass (which *could* carry it) never stored it, so `CoreComponentContext.ts` hardcoded `-1` for that category with a comment claiming "a JS Error has no equivalent" — true only for the native case, not for this port's own `Exception`. Added an `id` getter to `Exception` and wired both `CoreComponentContext.ts` call sites to use `e instanceof Exception ? e.id : -1`.
  - **`TextLabelController.ts::refresh()` never dispatched a synthetic `WE_RESIZED`** when a text-content change left the label the same size it already was (so the normal resize-triggered dispatch never fires) — AS3 explicitly re-dispatches in that case unless the refresh was itself resize-triggered, and the sibling `TextController.ts::refresh()` already has this exact logic; `TextLabelController` was just missing it. Ported directly from the sibling implementation.
  - **`ICoreWindowManager.notify()`/`confirm()`, `HabboNavigator`/`LegacyNavigator`'s `getButton()`/`refreshButton()`, `CallForHelpManager.reportSelfie()`/`reportPhoto()`, `GuideHelpManager.createHelpRequest()`, `HabboHelp.showWelcomeScreen()`** — confirmed pre-existing, already-documented-elsewhere stub/unwired states (no `ICoreWindowManager` implementer exists yet; the `help/` subsystem classes track fields but never send composer messages), not new gaps from this pass. Left as `TODO(AS3)`-commented stubs with unused params prefixed `_` rather than implemented, since each needs its own scoped investigation (composer wire formats, `WindowContext`-style completion-callback wiring) beyond a lint pass's scope.
  - **ESLint config gaps** (as opposed to code bugs): `@typescript-eslint/naming-convention`'s `method` selector had no `leadingUnderscore` allowance for private/protected members, so the ~90 bound event-handler methods written as arrow-function class fields (`private _onClick = (e) => {...}`, mixed with ~259 handlers *without* the underscore — both patterns are genuine, pre-existing convention, not a violation) all failed; and there was no exception for AS3 obfuscated placeholders (`_SafeStr_N`) or verbatim-AS3 snake_case field names (`DefaultAttStruct.width_min` etc.), which must never be renamed per the traceability mandate. Fixed the rule config directly (`eslint.config.mjs`) instead of papering over each site with per-line `eslint-disable` comments (several of which already existed and are now removed as dead).
  - Everything else (~275 of the ~347 errors) was mechanical: unused-but-intentional stub parameters prefixed `_` (matching this file's own established convention), `Function`-typed callbacks replaced with concrete signatures where one real call site existed or `(...args: any[]) => any` where the callback registry is genuinely polymorphic (`IWindow.addEventListener`, matching AS3's own untyped `Function`), inline `import('./X').Y` type annotations converted to top-level `import type`, and dead `let x = 0` initializers removed where every code path reassigns before the first read (verified via TypeScript's definite-assignment checking, not just visual inspection — two cases in `PlaneMaterialCellColumn.ts`/`PlaneMaterialCellMatrix.ts` turned out to be AS3 itself computing an overflow-adjusted offset that's then unconditionally discarded before its only use, confirmed byte-for-byte against AS3 rather than "improved").
  - Full repo: `pnpm build` (tsc + vite) and `pnpm lint` both clean (0 errors, 295 pre-existing `no-explicit-any` warnings untouched).
- 🐛 **Wheel scrolling jumped straight to the top or the bottom, never in between (found 2026-07-29, live user report: same symptom in the client's windows and in vortex-glaze).** Two independent causes, one per code path.
  - **The DOM's `deltaY` was fed in as if it were Flash's `MouseEvent.delta`.** Flash reports the wheel in *lines* (3 per notch on Windows) and every consumer here is built on that unit — `SmoothScroller` multiplies a line by `DEFAULT_SCROLL_STEP` (25px), `ScrollBarController` maps one to 0.06 of its 0..1 offset. The DOM reports pixels (~100 per notch, `deltaMode` 0), so `App.ts::_onWheel()`/`CanvasSurface.ts::_onWheel()` passing `-e.deltaY` made one notch worth 100 lines = 2500px of scroll intent; on a 500px-of-travel list that overshoots `maxScrollV` 5× and clamps to 0 or 1. Measured against the compiled `SmoothScroller`: one notch moved the full 500px before, 75px after. Added `core/window/utils/NativeWheelDelta.ts` (no AS3 counterpart — AS3 never needed one) converting pixel/line/page deltas to Flash lines at 3 per notch, so the ported chain reproduces the original client's feel rather than an invented one, and both entry points now use it. Trackpads, which emit many small pixel deltas instead of notches, come out proportionally small and scroll smoothly.
  - **`ScrollBarController`'s own wheel handling was still the pre-2026 fixed-step jump.** The port traced to `win63_version`, where both wheel branches (`update()` and `scrollButtonEventProc()`) test `delta > 0` and add/subtract a flat `_scrollStep` (0.1 = 10% of the range) with no animation — so with the cursor over the scrollbar itself, every wheel event was a 10% teleport regardless of magnitude. The primary tree replaced this with a `SmoothScroller` (`new SmoothScroller(getScrollOffset, setSmoothScrollOffset, getSmoothScrollAxisSize, 200, 60, true, null, 15)`), both branches calling `scrollWithWheel(delta)`, plus `setScrollPosition()`'s third `fromSmoothScroller` parameter feeding external position changes into `adjustStartPosition()`. Ported all of it. Kept AS3's oddity verbatim: `getSmoothScrollAxisSize()` is a bare `25 / _scrollStep` even though the scroller is constructed with a step of 15, so one wheel line moves 0.06 of the offset, not `_scrollStep` — documented at the declaration rather than "corrected". Also ported the `scrollable` setter's missing `setScrollPosition(scrollH/scrollV, false)` (without it `_offset` stays 0 while the target is scrolled, and `_offset` is where the smooth scroller starts from).
  - **Known remaining divergence, deliberately not changed**: the primary tree's increment/decrement buttons and track paging use a literal `SCROLL_STEP_SIZE` (15) where this port (following `win63_version`) uses the target's own `scrollStepH`/`scrollStepV`. Unrelated to the wheel and affects a different interaction, so it's flagged rather than folded into this fix.
- 🐛 **`HabboMessages.ts` had `GetBonusRareInfoMessageComposer` registered at wire header 957** (found via a real emulator log: `GiveSupplementToPetMessageParser` repeatedly throwing `"Not enough data: need 4, remaining 0"` for packet 957). Ground truth (`vortex-client/HabboMessages.as`, `sources/win63_version/habbo/communication/class_1881.as`, and the emulator's `Turbo.Revisions/Revision20260112/Headers.cs`) all agree 957 is `GiveSupplementToPetMessageComposer`'s header and `GetBonusRareInfoMessageComposer`'s real header is 75. Since `GetBonusRareInfoMessageComposer` sends an empty body and fires automatically from `BonusRarePromoWidget.ts` on every landing-view load, every such load sent an empty packet tagged 957, which the server routed to the pet-supplement parser expecting two ints. Fixed the header (957 → 75) and ported the previously entirely-missing `GiveSupplementToPetMessageComposer.ts` (`petId`, `supplementType` — AS3 only exists in `sources/win63_version/habbo/communication/messages/outgoing/room/engine/`, not the primary tree), registered at 957. No UI calls it yet — giving pets supplements isn't wired up anywhere in the client (no `PetSupplementEnum` port, no pet-care UI action) — this was a protocol-registry-only fix, not a new feature.

This documentation update is based on read-only filesystem counts. No TypeScript build was run for this docs-only change.

To refresh the snapshot manually:

```powershell
(Get-ChildItem sources\WIN63-202607011411-782849652 -Recurse -Filter *.as -File).Count
(Get-ChildItem sources\win63_version -Recurse -Filter *.as -File).Count
(Get-ChildItem sources\PRODUCTION-201601012205-226667486 -Recurse -Filter *.as -File).Count
(Get-ChildItem packages\vortex-engine\src -Recurse -Filter *.ts -File).Count
(Get-ChildItem packages\vortex-client\src -Recurse -Filter *.ts -File).Count
```
